In Him do those who trust put their trust.’”

And He, exalted be He, says:

“Say, ‘To whom belong the earth and whoever is in it, if you know?’”

“They will say, ‘To Allah.’ Say, ‘Will you not then take heed?’”

“Say, ‘Who is the Lord of the seven heavens and the Lord of the Mighty Throne?’”

“They will say, ‘To Allah.’ Say, ‘Will you not then fear Him?’”

The passage continues to His words:

“How, then, are you deluded?”

And then to His words:

“Allah has not taken any son, nor is there any god alongside Him. Otherwise, each god would have taken away what he created, and some would have prevailed over others. Exalted is Allah above what they attribute to Him!”

And He says:

“Most of them do not believe in Allah without associating partners with Him.”

From this and other evidence, we can recognize the error that has arisen concerning what is meant by affirming Allah’s oneness, for most

---

## Page 990

speculative theologians who expound Allah’s oneness in their books of theology and rational inquiry go no further than dividing it into (three types). They say: He is one in His essence, having no parts; one in His attributes, having no likeness; and one in His actions, having no partner.

The best-known of these three types among them is the third, namely “oneness in actions,” which means that the Creator of the world is one. They argue for this using what they call the argument of mutual hindrance and other proofs. They suppose that this is the affirmation of Allah’s oneness that is required, and that this is the meaning of our statement, “There is no god but Allah.” Indeed, they may even define divinity as the power to bring things into existence.

It is well known that the Arab polytheists to whom Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was first sent did not disagree with him on this. Rather, they acknowledged that Allah is the Creator of everything. They even acknowledged divine decree, yet remained polytheists.

It has thus become clear that no one in the world disputes the basis of this polytheism. The most that can be said, however, is that some people have held that certain existing things were created by someone other than Allah, such as the Qadariyya and others. Yet these people acknowledge that Allah created His servants and their ability, even if they say that the servants created their own actions.

Likewise, the adherents of philosophy, naturalism, and astrology who hold that some created beings bring certain things into existence, while acknowledging the Maker, regard these agents as themselves fashioned

---

## Page 991

and created; they do not claim that these agents are independent of the Creator or share with Him in creation. As for anyone who denies the Maker, he rejects and denies the Maker’s existence, as in the claim Pharaoh openly professed. Our discussion here, however, concerns those who associate partners with Allah while acknowledging His existence. These polytheists do not dispute the oneness that those theologians have established. Rather, they acknowledge it while remaining polytheists, as established by the Quran, the Sunnah, and scholarly consensus, and as necessarily known from the religion of Islam.

The same applies to the “second type”—their statement that “He has no likeness in His attributes.” No nation has affirmed a being without beginning equal to Him in His essence, whether it claimed that such a being acts jointly with Him or that it does not act at all. Rather, anyone who likens something of His creation to Him does so only in certain respects. Reason establishes that no created being can be His equal, sharing with Him everything that is necessary, possible, or impossible with respect to Him. That would entail affirming two contradictories at once, as discussed earlier.

Reason also establishes that any two self-subsisting beings must have something in common, such as both falling under the concepts of existence, self-subsistence, essence, and the like. Denying this entails total negation. The distinctive properties of lordship must also be affirmed. This has already been discussed.

Then the Jahmiyya, including the Muʿtazila and others, incorporated the denial of the divine attributes into what they called the affirmation of Allah’s oneness. Thus, if someone says that Allah has knowledge or power, that He will be seen in the Hereafter, or that the Quran is Allah’s speech, sent down and uncreated, they say: “He likens Allah to His creation and does not affirm His oneness.”

---

## Page 992

And the extremists among the philosophers and the Qarmatians went further, denying His most beautiful names and saying: “Whoever says that Allah is All-Knowing, All-Powerful, Almighty, and All-Wise likens Him to His creation and does not affirm His oneness.” The most extreme among these extremists went further still, saying: “He cannot be described through either negation or affirmation, because both entail likening Him to something else.”

All of them fell into a form of likening far worse than what they sought to escape: they likened Him to impossible things, nonexistent things, and inanimate objects, seeking to avoid what they claimed was likening Him to living beings. Yet it is well known that these attributes, which are affirmed for Allah, do not belong to Him in the same manner as they belong to any created being whatsoever. He—glorified and exalted is He—has nothing like Him in His essence, His attributes, or His actions. Thus, there is no difference between affirming His essence and affirming His attributes. If affirming His essence does not entail affirming its likeness to other essences, then affirming His attributes does not entail affirming any likeness to Him in those attributes. These Jahmiyya, who deny the divine attributes, therefore call this the affirmation of Allah’s oneness, describe its opposite as likening Him to His creation, and call themselves those who affirm His oneness.

The same applies to the “third type,” namely their statement: “He is one, with no division in His essence,” or “He has no part,” or “He has no portion.” This wording is ambiguous. For Allah—glorified is He—is “One, the Eternal Refuge. He neither begets nor is born, and there is none equal to Him.” It is therefore impossible for Him to be divided, to be broken into parts, or to have been composed of parts. Yet they include within this wording the denial that He is above His Throne, separate from His creation, and distinct from them, along with other such meanings that entail negating Him and denying His attributes. They count this as part of affirming His oneness.

---

## Page 993

It has thus become clear that what they call the affirmation of Allah’s oneness contains both truth and falsehood. Even if it were all true, the polytheists’ acknowledgment of all of it would not free them from the polytheism with which Allah described them in the Quran and for which the Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—fought them. Rather, they must acknowledge that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.

What is meant by (a deity) is not one who has the power to bring things into existence, as some leading speculative theologians supposed. They thought that divinity meant having this power to the exclusion of all others, and that whoever acknowledged that Allah alone has the power to bring things into existence had thereby testified that there is no deity except Him. Yet the polytheists acknowledged this while remaining polytheists, as explained earlier. Rather, the true deity is the One who deserves to be worshipped. He is a deity in the sense of one who is worshipped, not one who worships. Affirming Allah’s oneness means worshipping Allah alone, without any partner; associating partners with Him means setting up another deity alongside Allah.

And if it becomes clear that the utmost these speculative thinkers establish—those who affirm divine decree and identify themselves with the Sunnah—is merely Allah’s oneness in His lordship, and that Allah is the Lord of everything, even though the polytheists acknowledged this while remaining polytheists, then the same applies to certain groups of Sufis and those who profess spiritual knowledge, realization, and the affirmation of Allah’s oneness. The utmost they attain in affirming His oneness is to witness this oneness: to witness that Allah is the Lord, Sovereign, and Creator of everything, especially when the mystic becomes so absorbed in the One he contemplates that he loses awareness of

---

## Page 994

his own existence, so absorbed in the One he witnesses that he loses awareness of his own witnessing, and so absorbed in the One he knows that he loses awareness of his own knowledge. He enters a state of self-effacement in contemplating Allah’s oneness in His lordship, such that the one who once did not exist fades from awareness, while the One who has always existed remains. In their view, this is the ultimate goal, beyond which there is no further goal. Yet it is well known that this merely realizes the oneness that the polytheists acknowledged. This affirmation of oneness alone does not make a person a Muslim, let alone a close friend of Allah or one of His foremost close friends.

One group among those devoted to Sufism and spiritual knowledge affirms this oneness while also affirming the divine attributes. They experience self-effacement in contemplating Allah’s oneness in His lordship, while affirming that the world has a Creator who is distinct from His creation. Others combine this with the denial of the divine attributes, thus also falling into the negation of Allah’s attributes. This is worse than the condition of many polytheists.

Jahm denied the divine attributes and held that human actions are compelled. Thus, this is the realization of Jahm’s doctrine. However, if he affirms commands and prohibitions, reward and punishment, he differs from the polytheists in this respect. Yet Jahm and his followers exclude deeds from faith, so commands and prohibitions, reward and punishment carry less weight in his doctrine.

The Najjāriyya, the Dirāriyya, and others are close to Jahm in matters of divine decree and faith, while also approaching his position in denying the divine attributes.

---

## Page 995

The Kullābiyya and the Ash'ariyya are better than these groups regarding the divine attributes: they affirm for Allah the attributes known through reason, and their leading scholars broadly affirm the attributes known through transmitted revelation, as their positions have been detailed elsewhere. As for divine decree and questions of religious designations and their associated rulings, their positions are close to one another.

The Kullābiyya are the followers of Abū Muhammad 'Abd Allāh ibn Sa'īd ibn Kullāb, whose approach al-Ash'arī followed. Ibn Kullāb’s followers, such as al-Hārith al-Muhāsibī, Abū al-'Abbās al-Qalānisī, and others like them, are better than the Ash'ariyya in both respects. The closer a person is to the early generations and the leading scholars, the sounder and better his position is.

The Karrāmiyya hold a reprehensible position on faith, one in which no one preceded them: they define faith as a verbal declaration, even without the heart’s affirmation. Thus, they regard the hypocrite as a believer, yet hold that he will remain in the Fire forever. They therefore differ from the mainstream Muslim community in designation, but not in the ruling. Regarding the divine attributes, divine decree, and the threat of punishment, however, they are closer to the Sunnah than most schools of speculative theology whose positions depart from it.

As for the Mu'tazila, they deny the divine attributes and approach Jahm’s position, but they

---

## Page 996

deny divine decree. Although they attach great importance to the commands and prohibitions, the promise of reward and the threat of punishment—and go to excess in this—they deny divine decree and thus have an element of associating partners with Allah in this respect.

Acknowledging the commands and prohibitions, the promise of reward and the threat of punishment while denying divine decree is better than acknowledging divine decree while denying the commands and prohibitions, the promise of reward and the threat of punishment. Hence, during the time of the Companions and the Successors, there was no one who denied these commands, prohibitions, promises, and threats, although the Qadariyya had emerged among them, as had the Khawārij—the Harūriyya. The religious innovations that first appear are those whose error is less apparent. The weaker those who uphold the light of prophethood become, the stronger religious innovation grows.

Thus, these Sufis who witness the reality of Allah’s universal lordship while disregarding His commands and prohibitions are worse than the Qadariyya of the Mu'tazila and others like them. The latter resemble the Magians, whereas these Sufis resemble the polytheists who said:

“If Allah had willed, we would not have associated partners with Him, nor would our fathers, nor would we have forbidden anything.”

The polytheists are worse than the Magians. This, then, is a momentous principle that every Muslim must understand, for it is the foundation of Islam by which the basis of faith is distinguished from what the unbelievers follow: belief in Allah’s oneness and in His Messenger’s mission, expressed in the testimony that none is worthy of worship except Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah.

Many people have failed to uphold the true meaning of these two foundations, or one of them, while imagining that they have attained the utmost realization of truth, monotheism, knowledge, and spiritual understanding.

---

## Page 997

Thus, a polytheist’s acknowledgment that Allah is the Lord, Sovereign, and Creator of all things does not save him from Allah’s punishment unless it is accompanied by his acknowledgment that none is worthy of worship except Allah—so no one but Him deserves worship—and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah, whose reports must therefore be believed and whose commands must be obeyed. These two foundations must therefore be discussed.

The First Foundation: “Allah’s Oneness in Worship”

As noted earlier, Allah, glorified is He, has told us that the polytheists established intermediaries between themselves and Allah, invoking them and taking them as intercessors without Allah’s permission. Allah Most High says:

“They worship besides Allah what can neither harm nor benefit them, and say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Are you informing Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on earth?’ Glorified and exalted is He above what they associate with Him!”

He thus declares that those who took these beings as intercessors are polytheists. Allah Most High also relates the words of the believer mentioned in Surah Yā-Sīn:

“Why should I not worship the One who created me, and to whom you will be returned?

“Should I take gods besides Him? If the Most Merciful intends any harm for me, their intercession will avail me nothing, nor can they rescue me.

“I would then surely be in manifest error.

“I have believed in your Lord, so listen to me.”

Allah Most High says:

“You have come to Us alone, just as We created you the first time, leaving behind all that We bestowed upon you. We do not see with you your intercessors, whom you claimed to be partners in your affairs. The bonds between you have been severed, and those you used to claim have forsaken you.”

He, glorified is He, thus tells us that they claimed their intercessors to be partners in their affairs. Allah Most High says:

“Have they taken intercessors besides Allah? Say, ‘Even though they possess nothing and have no understanding?’

“Say, ‘All intercession belongs to Allah. His is the dominion of the heavens and the earth. Then to Him you will be returned.’”

Allah Most High says: “You have no

---

## Page 998

protector or intercessor besides Him.” Allah Most High says:

“And warn with it those who fear being gathered before their Lord, for they will have no protector or intercessor besides Him.”

Allah Most High says:

“Who can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

Allah Most High says:

“They say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken a son.’ Glory be to Him! Rather, they are honored servants.

“They do not speak before He does, and they act at His command.

“He knows what lies before them and what lies behind them. They intercede only for those with whom He is pleased, and they tremble in awe of Him.”

Allah Most High says:

“How many angels there are in the heavens whose intercession avails nothing unless Allah first grants permission for whomever He wills and is pleased with.”

Allah Most High says:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim besides Allah. They possess not even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth. They have no share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.’

“Intercession with Him is of no benefit except for one to whom He grants permission.”

Allah Most High says:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim besides Him. They have no power to remove your affliction or to shift it elsewhere.’

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, striving to be closest to Him. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Surely your Lord’s punishment is to be feared.”

A group of the early generations said: Some people used to invoke Ezra, the Messiah, and the angels. Allah therefore revealed this verse, explaining that the angels and prophets seek to draw near to Allah, hope for His mercy, and fear His punishment.

Truly upholding Allah’s oneness includes knowing that Allah Most High has established for Himself a right in which no created being shares, such as worship, reliance, fear, reverent awe, and God-consciousness. As Allah Most High says:

“Do not set up another god alongside Allah, lest you be left condemned and forsaken.”

Allah Most High says: “Indeed, We have sent down

---

## Page 999

to you the Book in truth, so worship Allah, devoting your religion sincerely to Him.” Allah Most High says:

“Say, ‘I have been commanded to worship Allah, devoting my religion sincerely to Him.’”

Allah Most High says:

“Say, ‘Is it other than Allah that you command me to worship, O ignorant ones?’”

Through to His words: “the grateful.”

Each messenger says to his people:

“Worship Allah; you have no god other than Him.”

Regarding reliance, Allah Most High says:

“And put your trust in Allah, if you are believers.”

“And in Allah let the believers put their trust.”

He says:

“Say, ‘Allah is sufficient for me. In Him those who trust put their trust.’”

Allah Most High says:

“Had they been content with what Allah and His Messenger gave them, and said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us. Allah will give us of His bounty, and so will His Messenger. To Allah we turn with longing.’”

Thus, regarding giving, He says: “what Allah and His Messenger gave them.” But regarding reliance, He says: “and said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us,’” without adding “and His Messenger.” For giving here means giving in accordance with revealed law. This includes the permissions and declarations of lawfulness conveyed by the Messenger: what is lawful is what he has declared lawful, what is unlawful is what he has forbidden, and religion is what he has prescribed. Allah Most High says:

“Whatever the Messenger gives you, take it; and whatever he forbids you, refrain from it.”

As for being sufficient, this means being enough; Allah alone suffices His servant. As Allah Most High says:

“Those to whom people said, ‘The people have gathered against you, so fear them,’ but this only increased them in faith, and they said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.’”

He alone is sufficient for them all. Allah Most High says:

“O Prophet, Allah is sufficient for you and for the believers who follow you.”

That is, Allah is sufficient for you and for those believers who follow you: He suffices for you all.

---

## Page 1000

And it does not mean that Allah and the believers together are sufficient for you, as some mistakenly suppose. He alone suffices His Prophet; He is sufficient for him, and no one shares with Him in sufficing the Messenger. This linguistic construction is like the poet’s words:

“A sword of Indian steel suffices for you and al-Dahhāk.”

The Arabs also say: “A dirham suffices for you and Zayd,” meaning that one dirham suffices for both of you.

Regarding fear, reverent awe, and mindfulness of Allah, He says:

“Whoever obeys Allah and His Messenger, fears Allah and is mindful of Him—it is they who will triumph.”

Thus, He affirms obedience to both Allah and the Messenger, but reserves reverent awe and mindfulness for Allah alone. Likewise, Noah, peace be upon him, said:

“Indeed, I am a clear warner to you.”

“Worship Allah, be mindful of Him, and obey me.”

He therefore directs worship and mindfulness to Allah alone, and obedience to the Messenger; for whoever obeys the Messenger has obeyed Allah.

Allah Most High says:

“So do not fear people, but fear Me.”

Allah Most High also says:

“So do not fear them, but fear Me, if you are believers.”

Abraham, the close friend of Allah, peace be upon him, said:

“How could I fear what you associate with Allah, while you do not fear associating with Allah that for which He has sent down no authority to you? Which of the two parties has more right to security, if you know?”

“Those who believe and do not mix their faith with wrongdoing—it is they who will have security, and they are rightly guided.”

In the two Sahīh collections, Ibn Masʿūd is reported to have said: When this verse was revealed, it troubled the Companions of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and they said: “Which of us has not wronged himself?”

---

## Page 1001

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, replied:

“It refers only to associating partners with Allah. Have you not heard the words of the righteous servant: ‘Indeed, associating partners with Allah is a tremendous wrong’?”

Allah Most High says:

“So fear Me alone.”

“And be mindful of Me alone.”

Another example of this is that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would say in his sermons:

“Whoever obeys Allah and His Messenger is rightly guided. Whoever disobeys them harms only himself and will not harm Allah in the least.”

He also said:

“And do not say, ‘What Allah wills and Muhammad wills,’ but say, ‘What Allah wills, then what Muhammad wills.’”

Thus, when speaking of obedience, he joined the Messenger’s name to Allah’s with the conjunction “and”; but when speaking of the will, he instructed that “then” be used. This is because obedience to the Messenger is obedience to Allah: whoever obeys the Messenger has obeyed Allah, and obedience to Allah is obedience to the Messenger. The will, however, is different: no servant’s will is Allah’s will, nor does Allah’s will necessarily entail the will of His servants. Rather, whatever Allah wills happens, even if people do not will it; and whatever people will does not happen unless Allah wills it.

The Second Principle: The Rights of the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace

We must therefore believe in him, obey him, follow him, please him, love him, submit to his judgment, and the like

---

## Page 1002

thereof. Allah Most High says:

“Whoever obeys the Messenger has indeed obeyed Allah.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Allah and His Messenger are more worthy of being pleased.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Say: If your fathers, your sons, your brothers, your spouses, your relatives, the wealth you have acquired, the trade whose decline you fear, and the homes you cherish are dearer to you than Allah, His Messenger, and striving in His cause, then wait until Allah brings about His command.”

Allah Most High also says:

“But no, by your Lord, they will not believe until they make you judge concerning the disputes that arise among them, then find within themselves no discomfort over what you have decided and submit completely.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Say: If you love Allah, then follow me; Allah will love you.”

And there are other similar passages.

---

## Page 1003

Section

Once this is established, it is well known that one must believe in Allah’s creation and command: in His decree and His revealed law.

The misguided who delve into divine decree have divided into three groups: the Magian group, the polytheistic group, and the followers of Iblīs.

The Magian group consists of those who deny Allah’s decree, even though they believe in His commands and prohibitions. Their extremists deny His foreknowledge and the written decree, while their less extreme adherents deny the all-encompassing scope of His will, creation, and power. These are the Muʿtazilah and those who agree with them.

The second group, the polytheistic group, consists of those who acknowledge divine ordainment and decree but deny commands and prohibitions. Allah Most High says:

“Those who associate others with Allah will say, ‘If Allah had willed, neither we nor our fathers would have associated others with Him, nor would we have forbidden anything.’”

Whoever invokes divine decree to justify setting aside commands and prohibitions belongs to this group. This has become widespread among those Sufis who claim to have attained spiritual reality.

The third group, the followers of Iblīs, consists of those who acknowledge both, but regard them as a contradiction on the part of the Lord—glorified and exalted is He—and impugn His wisdom and justice. Such views are related of Iblīs, their leader, as recorded by writers on sectarian doctrines and transmitted from the People of the Book.

---

## Page 1004

The point is that these are fabrications of those who have gone astray. As for those who are guided and attain success, they believe in both. They believe that Allah is the Creator, Lord, and Sovereign of everything; that whatever He wills comes to pass, and whatever He does not will does not come to pass; that He has power over all things, encompasses everything in His knowledge, and has recorded everything in a clear Record. This principle affirms Allah’s knowledge, power, will, oneness, and lordship, and that He is the Creator, Lord, and Sovereign of everything—all of which are fundamental principles of faith.

Yet they do not deny the causes Allah has created, through which He creates their effects. As He Most High says:

“Until, when they carry heavy clouds, We drive them to a lifeless land, then send down water through it, and with that water bring forth fruits of every kind.”

And He Most High says:

“Through it Allah guides those who follow what pleases Him to the ways of peace.”

And He Most High says:

“He causes many to go astray through it, and guides many through it.”

He thus tells us that He acts through causes. Whoever says that He acts when they occur, but not through them, contradicts what the Quran teaches and denies the powers and natural properties Allah has created. This is akin to denying the powers Allah has created in living beings, through which they act, such as a human being’s capacity to act. Likewise, whoever regards these powers as themselves the creators of those effects has associated partners with Allah and attributed His action to another.

For every cause depends upon another cause for its effect to occur. There must also be an impediment that prevents it from producing its effect unless Allah removes that impediment. Thus, there is nothing in

---

## Page 1005

existence that can bring anything about independently whenever it wills, except Allah alone. He Most High says:

“And of everything We have created pairs, so that you may take heed.”

That is, so that you may know that the Creator of all pairs is One. Thus, anyone who says, “Only one thing can proceed from Allah, because only one thing can proceed from the One,” is ignorant. For there is no single being in existence that has produced anything by itself—neither one thing nor two—except Allah, who created all pairs: of what the earth grows, of themselves, and of things they do not know.

Fire, in which Allah has created heat, only causes burning when there is both fire and something susceptible to being burned. If it falls upon a salamander, a ruby, or something similar, it does not burn them. A body may also be coated with something that prevents it from burning. Likewise, the rays emitted by the sun require a body upon which they can be reflected. If a barrier, such as a cloud or a roof, intervenes, the rays do not reach beneath it. This has been explained at length elsewhere.

The point here is that “belief in divine decree” is indispensable, for belief in divine decree completes belief in Allah’s oneness. As Ibn ‘Abbās said: “It is what holds belief in Allah’s oneness together. Whoever affirms Allah’s oneness and believes in divine decree has perfected his belief in His oneness; whoever affirms Allah’s oneness but denies divine decree has undone his belief in His oneness.”

Belief in the revealed law is also indispensable: belief in the commands and prohibitions, the promises and warnings, with which Allah sent His messengers and revealed His Books.

---

## Page 1006

And human beings cannot do without a law to live by in this world, for they must act to secure benefit and act to ward off harm. The revealed law distinguishes between actions that benefit them and actions that harm them. It is Allah’s justice within His creation and His light among His servants. Human beings therefore cannot live without a law by which they distinguish what they should do from what they should refrain from doing.

What is meant by law is not merely justice between people in their dealings. Even a person living alone must do some things and refrain from others, for a human being is one who continually intends and acts. As the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The names that most truthfully describe their bearers are Hārith and Hammām.”

This is what is meant by describing a human being as acting through volition. When someone has a will, he acts upon it, and he must know whether what he wants is beneficial or harmful to him, and whether it makes him better or corrupts him.

People know some of this through their innate disposition, just as they know that eating and drinking benefit them, and just as their innate disposition gives them self-evident knowledge. Others know it through inference, as with the conclusions to which their reason guides them. Some of it, however, they know only through the messengers’ teaching, explanation, and guidance.

It is in this connection that people have debated whether the goodness and badness of actions can be known by reason, or whether actions have no goodness or badness that can be known by reason. This has been discussed at length elsewhere, and we have explained the confusion that has arisen concerning this question. They agree that whether an act is congenial or repugnant to the one who performs it can be known by reason, which means

---

## Page 1007

that the agent brings about what he loves and enjoys, and what he hates and what harms him. This much may be known through reason, through the revealed law, or through both together.

However, detailed knowledge of this, and knowledge of the ultimate outcome of actions—happiness or misery in the Hereafter—can be obtained only through revelation. People cannot know through their reason the details of the Last Day that the messengers have conveyed, or the detailed prescriptions of the revealed laws that they have commanded. Likewise, people cannot know through their reason the details of Allah’s names and attributes conveyed by the messengers, although they may know their general outlines through reason. This detailed knowledge, through which faith is attained and which the Book has brought, is indicated by the words of the Most High:

“And thus We have revealed to you an inspiration by Our command. You did not know what the Book was, nor what faith was, but We made it a light by which We guide whom We will of Our servants.”

And His words:

“Say, ‘If I go astray, I go astray only to my own detriment; but if I am guided, it is through what my Lord reveals to me. Indeed, He is All-Hearing, Near.’”

And His words:

“Say, ‘I warn you only through revelation.’”

Yet one group mistakenly supposed that goodness and badness had a meaning other than this, and that this meaning could be known through reason. Opposing them was another group, who supposed that the goodness and badness taught by the revealed law fell outside this understanding. Both groups—those who affirmed goodness and badness as known through reason, and those who affirmed them as known through the revealed law, while excluding them from this category—were mistaken.

---

## Page 1008

Moreover, since both groups denied that Allah is described as possessing love, pleasure, displeasure, joy, and similar attributes conveyed by the divine texts and supported by rational evidence, they disagreed—despite agreeing that Allah does nothing that would be bad on His part. They held two views on whether such an act is intrinsically impossible, whether it is inconceivable that He could have the power to do anything bad, and whether He—glorified be He—is above such acts and refrains from them solely because of the badness discernible by reason that they had posited.

Both views deviate in the same manner as the two views mentioned earlier. The first group drew no distinction, in His creation and command, between guidance and misguidance, obedience and disobedience, the righteous and the wicked, the people of Paradise and the people of Hell, or mercy and punishment. Thus, they did not regard Him as deserving praise for the justice He exercises or the injustice He refrains from, nor for the kindness and blessings He bestows or the torment and retribution He withholds. The other group declared Him above such acts on the basis of the badness discernible by reason that they had posited, which has no reality. They placed Him on the same footing as His creatures regarding what is good and bad, and likened Him to His servants regarding what He commands and forbids.

Whoever considers divine decree alone, extols absorption in the affirmation of Allah’s sole lordship, and stops at the reality of His governance of creation does not distinguish between knowledge and ignorance, truthfulness and falsehood, righteousness and wickedness, justice and injustice, obedience and disobedience, guidance and misguidance, right conduct and waywardness, Allah’s allies and His enemies, or the people of Paradise and the people of Hell. Although these people indisputably contradict Allah’s Books, His religion, and His revealed laws, they are

---

## Page 1009

also at odds with the self-evident truths of sensory perception and direct experience, and of reason and inference. Each of them inevitably takes pleasure in some things and suffers pain from others. He therefore distinguishes between what he eats and drinks and what he does not, and between heat and cold that harm him and those that do not. This distinction between what benefits him and what harms him constitutes the reality of revealed law and religion.

Whoever supposes that a human being can reach a point at which these two are always equal to him has made a false claim and contradicted the self-evident evidence of the senses. A person may, however, occasionally experience a condition such as intoxication, unconsciousness, or something similar that prevents him from perceiving certain things. But for his perception to cease entirely while he remains alive is impossible. Even a sleeping person has not lost his own awareness: in his dreams he sometimes sees things that distress him and at other times things that delight him.

Thus, the states described as overwhelming absorption, self-effacement, intoxication, and the like involve only a lack of awareness of some things rather than others. Although they reflect a deficiency in the person experiencing them—because his discernment is impaired—they never reach a point at which discernment disappears altogether. Whoever denies all discernment in this spiritual station and exalts it has misunderstood both the reality of creation and the reality of religion: divine decree and revealed law. He has misunderstood Allah’s creation and His command, supposing that such a state exists when it has no existence, and supposing it to be praiseworthy when there is nothing praiseworthy in lacking discernment—that is, reason and knowledge.

If you hear some spiritual teachers say, “I wish to have no wishes,” or that one who knows Allah has no interest of his own and becomes like a corpse in the hands of the person washing it, or something similar, this is praiseworthy only with respect to

---

## Page 1010

the relinquishment of the will he is commanded to exercise, his having no personal interest that he is not commanded to pursue, and his being like a corpse in not seeking what he has not been commanded to seek and not warding off what he has not been commanded to ward off. Whoever means by this that his will ceases altogether and that he no longer perceives pleasure and pain, benefit and harm, contradicts the self-evident truths of sensory perception and reason. Whoever praises this contradicts the self-evident truths of religion and reason.

Self-effacement is used in three senses:

The first is self-effacement in accordance with religion and revealed law, which the messengers brought and the Scriptures conveyed. It consists in abandoning what Allah has not commanded by doing what Allah has commanded. Thus, through worshipping Him, one abandons worship of anyone else; through obeying Him and His Messenger, one abandons obedience to anyone else; through relying upon Him, one abandons reliance upon anyone else; through loving Him and His Messenger, one abandons love of anything besides Him; and through fearing Him, one abandons fear of anyone else. In this way, the servant does not follow his desires without guidance from Allah, and Allah and His Messenger are dearer to him than all else. As the Most High says:

“Say, ‘If your fathers, your sons, your brothers, your spouses, your relatives, the wealth you have acquired, the trade whose decline you fear, and the homes you cherish are dearer to you than Allah and His Messenger and striving in His cause, then wait until Allah brings about His command.’”

All of this is what Allah and His Messenger have commanded. As for (the second kind of self-effacement: it is the kind described by some Sufis, in which one loses awareness of everything other than Allah Most High. Absorption in the One he worships makes him lose awareness of his worship, and absorption in the One he remembers makes him lose awareness of his remembrance

---

## Page 1011

and absorption in the One he knows makes him lose awareness of his own knowing, to the point that he may lose awareness of his own perception of anything other than Allah Most High. This is an imperfect state that may arise in some who travel the path to Allah; it is not an essential part of that path. Hence, nothing like this is known to have occurred to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, or to the earliest believers. Whoever regards this as the ultimate attainment of those travelling the path is manifestly astray. Likewise, whoever regards it as an essential part of the path to Allah is mistaken. Rather, it is an incidental state that arises in some people but not others, not something that necessarily occurs to everyone travelling the path.

The third kind is self-effacement that denies the existence of anything other than Allah: a person regards the existence of creation as identical to the existence of the Creator, and existence as one and the same reality. This is the doctrine of those who espouse heresy and the identity of Creator and creation, who are among the most misguided of Allah’s servants.

As for their contradiction of self-evident reason and logical inference, none of them can apply his position consistently. If someone contemplates divine decree without distinguishing between what is commanded and what is forbidden, and is then treated accordingly—for example, beaten and starved until he suffers severe illnesses and pains—then, if he blames and censures the person who does this to him, he has contradicted his own position and departed from the fundamental principle of his doctrine. He would be told: “What this person has done was decreed and ordained. Allah’s creation, decree and will encompass both you and him and apply to you both. If divine decree is a valid argument in your defence, then it is a valid argument in his defence too. Otherwise, it is a valid argument for neither you nor him.”

Self-evident reason thus demonstrates the invalidity of the position of anyone who considers divine decree and turns away

---

## Page 1012

from what is commanded and forbidden. The believer is commanded to do what is enjoined, refrain from what is prohibited, and patiently endure what is decreed, as Allah Most High says:

“If you remain patient and mindful of Allah, their plotting will not harm you at all.”

And He says in the account of Joseph:

“Whoever is mindful of Allah and patient—surely Allah does not let the reward of those who do good go to waste.”

Being mindful of Allah means doing what Allah has commanded and refraining from what He has forbidden. Hence Allah Most High says:

“So be patient; surely Allah’s promise is true. Seek forgiveness for your sin, and glorify your Lord with praise in the evening and the morning.”

Thus, alongside seeking forgiveness, He commanded him to be patient. All servants, from the first to the last, must seek forgiveness. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said in an authentic hadith:

“O people, repent to your Lord! By the One in whose hand is my soul, I seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him more than seventy times a day.”

He also said:

“A cloud comes over my heart, and I seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him a hundred times a day.”

And he used to say:

“O Allah, forgive my sins, my ignorance, my excesses in my affairs, and whatever You know better than I do. O Allah, forgive what I have done mistakenly and deliberately, in jest and in earnest—all of these are found in me. O Allah, forgive what I have done in the past and what I may do hereafter, what I have concealed and what I have made known, and whatever You know better than I do. You are the One who brings forward, and You are the One who holds back.”

It is related that Adam, the father of humankind, sought his Lord’s forgiveness and repented to Him; his Lord then chose him, accepted his repentance, and guided him. And it is related that Iblīs, the father of the jinn—may Allah curse him—persisted in his disobedience, invoking divine decree as his justification; so Allah cursed him and cast him away. Whoever sins, repents, and feels remorse has thus taken after his father—and whoever takes after his father is not to be faulted.

---

## Page 1013

Allah Most High says:

“Humanity undertook it; indeed, humankind is ever unjust and ignorant.”

“So that Allah may punish the hypocrites, men and women, and the polytheists, men and women, and accept the repentance of the believers, men and women. Allah is ever All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”

For this reason, Allah, glorified be He, has paired worshipping Him alone with seeking forgiveness in several verses. Allah Most High says:

“So know that there is no god but Allah, and seek forgiveness for your sin and for the believing men and women.”

And He says:

“So be steadfast in turning to Him and seek His forgiveness.”

And He says:

“Alif Lām Rā. A Book whose verses have been perfected, then set out in detail, from One who is All-Wise, All-Aware.”

“Worship none but Allah. Indeed, I am a warner and a bearer of good news to you from Him.”

“And seek your Lord’s forgiveness, then turn to Him in repentance; He will grant you good enjoyment until an appointed time.”

In the hadith related by Ibn Abī ʿĀsim and others:

“Satan says: ‘I destroyed people through sins, but they destroyed me by declaring that there is no god but Allah and by seeking forgiveness. When I saw that, I spread religious deviations among them. Thus they sin without repenting, because they think they are doing good.’”

Allah, glorified be He, has related that Jonah called out in the darkness: “There is no god but You. Glory be to You! Indeed, I have been among the wrongdoers.” Allah Most High says:

“So We answered him and rescued him from distress. And thus do We rescue the believers.”

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“As for the supplication of my brother Jonah, no distressed person invokes it without Allah relieving his distress.”

In sum, the servant must adhere to two fundamental principles regarding divine command, and two regarding divine decree.

---

## Page 1014

Thus, regarding “divine command,” he must strive to obey, both in knowledge and in practice. You must therefore continually strive to learn what Allah has commanded and to act upon it. He must then seek forgiveness and repent for neglecting what he has been commanded to do and for overstepping the limits. For this reason, it is prescribed to conclude all deeds by seeking forgiveness. When the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, finished his prayer, he would seek forgiveness three times. Allah Most High says:

“And those who seek forgiveness before dawn.”

They prayed during the night and concluded it by seeking forgiveness. The last surah revealed was the saying of Allah Most High:

“When Allah’s help comes, and the victory,

and you see people entering Allah’s religion in crowds,

then glorify your Lord with praise and seek His forgiveness. Indeed, He is ever Accepting of repentance.”

It is related in the authentic hadith collection that he, peace and blessings be upon him, would frequently say while bowing and prostrating:

“Glory be to You, O Allah, our Lord, and praise be to You. O Allah, forgive me.”

In doing so, he was putting the Quran’s command into practice.

As for “divine decree,” he must seek Allah’s help in doing what He has commanded, rely upon Him, supplicate to Him, earnestly turn to Him, seek refuge in Him, and acknowledge his need for Him in seeking good and abandoning evil. He must patiently endure what has been decreed and know that whatever befell him could not have missed him, and whatever missed him could not have befallen him. When people harm him, he should know that this has been decreed for him.

The debate between Adam and Moses falls into this category, when Moses said:

“O Adam, you are the father of humanity. Allah created you with His Hand, breathed into you of His spirit, and made His angels prostrate before you. Why did you expel us

---

## Page 1015

and yourself from Paradise?”

Adam replied:

“You are Moses, whom Allah chose to speak to. How long before I was created did you find these words written against me: ‘And Adam disobeyed his Lord and went astray’?”

Moses answered, “Such and such a length of time beforehand.” Thus Adam prevailed over Moses in the argument. This was because Moses was not reproaching Adam for the sin itself: Adam had already repented of it, and one who repents of a sin is like one who has no sin. Rather, he was reproaching him for the calamity that had befallen them as a result.

They are commanded to consider divine decree when faced with calamities and to seek forgiveness for their faults, as Allah Most High says:

“So be patient; indeed, Allah’s promise is true. And seek forgiveness for your sin.”

Whoever observes both divine command and divine decree in the manner described worships Allah, obeys Him, seeks His help, and relies upon Him. He is among those whom Allah has blessed: the prophets, the truthful, the martyrs, and the righteous. What excellent companions they are!

Allah, glorified be He, has brought these two principles together in several passages, such as His saying:

“You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help.”

And His saying:

“So worship Him and rely upon Him.”

And His saying:

“Upon Him I rely, and to Him I turn.”

And His saying:

“Whoever fears Allah, He will provide a way out for him,”

“and provide for him from where he does not expect. And whoever relies upon Allah, He is sufficient for him. Indeed, Allah will accomplish His purpose. Allah has appointed a measure for everything.”

Worship, then, is for Allah, and help is sought from Him. “The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, used to say when offering a sacrifice:

---

## Page 1016

‘O Allah, this is from You and for You.’” Whatever is not through Allah cannot come to be, for there is no might or power except through Allah; and whatever is not through Allah brings no benefit and does not endure.

Worship of Him requires two fundamental principles: the first is to devote one’s religion sincerely to Him; the second is to conform to His command, with which He sent His messengers.

For this reason, ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb, may Allah be pleased with him, used to say in his supplication:

“O Allah, make all my deeds righteous, make them sincerely devoted to seeking Your Face, and let no one else have any share in them.”

Regarding Allah Most High’s words,

“that He may test you as to which of you is best in deed,”

al-Fudayl ibn ‘Iyād said, “The most sincere and the most correct.” They asked, “O Abū ‘Alī, what does ‘the most sincere and the most correct’ mean?” He replied:

“If a deed is sincere but not correct, it will not be accepted. If it is correct but not sincere, it will not be accepted until it is both sincere and correct. Sincerity means that it is done for Allah, and correctness means that it accords with the Sunnah.”

For this reason, Allah censured the polytheists in the Quran for following what their partners had prescribed for them in religion without Allah’s permission: worshipping others besides Him and engaging in religious practices He had not prescribed. As Allah Most High says:

“Or do they have partners who have prescribed for them in religion what Allah has not authorized?”

He likewise censured them for prohibiting what Allah had not prohibited. The true religion is that nothing is forbidden except what Allah has forbidden, and nothing belongs to religion except what He has prescribed.

People fall into four categories with respect to worshipping Him and seeking His help:

---

## Page 1017

The God-fearing believers act for Him and through Him: they worship Him and seek His help.

Another group worships Him without seeking His help or exercising patience. You find among them a concern for obedience, scrupulousness, and adherence to the Sunnah; yet they lack reliance upon Allah, seeking His help, and patience. Instead, they exhibit helplessness and anxiety.

Another group seeks His help, relies upon Him, and exercises patience, but does not steadfastly obey His commands or follow the Sunnah. One of them may be granted power and attain a certain inward and outward spiritual state. He may receive unveilings and abilities to bring about effects that the former group has not received. Yet he has no good final outcome, for he is not among the God-fearing, and the good final outcome belongs to those who fear Allah.

The former group has a weak religious commitment, but it endures and remains, provided the person does not undermine it through anxiety and helplessness. Those in the latter group possess a spiritual state and strength, but nothing of this remains for them except insofar as they obey the commands and follow the Sunnah.

The worst category consists of those who neither worship Him nor seek His help. Such a person does not recognize that his knowledge is for Allah or that it is through Allah.

The Mu‘tazilites and others like them—among the Qadarites who deny divine decree—are better in their reverence for the commands and prohibitions, and the promise of reward and threat of punishment, than these fatalist Qadarites who turn away from the revealed Law and its commands and prohibitions.

The Sufis are better than the Mu‘tazilites with respect to divine decree and witnessing Allah’s oneness in lordship. Yet among them are those who hold certain innovations while disregarding some of the commands and prohibitions, and the promise of reward and threat of punishment

---

## Page 1018

until they make witnessing Allah’s oneness in lordship, and fanāʾ (annihilation) in that witnessing, the ultimate goal. They also withdraw from the Muslim community and its Sunnah; in this respect, they too are Mu‘tazilites—those who withdraw. The innovation into which they have fallen may be worse than the innovation of those Mu‘tazilites. Both groups originated in Basra.

Allah’s religion is none other than that with which He sent His messengers and which He revealed in His Books. It is the straight path: the way of the Companions of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—the best generation, the finest of this community, and the most honored of creation in the sight of Allah Most High after the prophets.

The Most High says:

“And the first forerunners among the Emigrants and the Helpers and those who followed them with good conduct—Allah is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him.”

His pleasure with the first forerunners is unqualified, whereas His pleasure with those who follow them is conditional upon their following them in righteousness. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said in authentic hadiths:

“The best generation is the generation among whom I was sent, then those who follow them, then those who follow them.”

‘Abd Allāh ibn Mas‘ūd, may Allah be pleased with him, used to say:

“Whoever among you wishes to follow an example should follow those who have died, for the living are not safe from being put to trial. Those are the Companions of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: the most righteous in heart of this community, the deepest in knowledge, and the least given to affectation. They were people whom Allah chose to accompany His Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and to establish His religion. So recognize their due and hold fast to their guidance, for they followed upright guidance.”

---

## Page 1019

Hudhayfah ibn al-Yamān, may Allah be pleased with them both, also said:

“O reciters of the Quran, remain steadfast and follow the path of those who came before you. By Allah, if you follow them, you will have forged far ahead; but if you turn to the right or the left, you will have strayed far indeed.”

‘Abd Allāh ibn Mas‘ūd, may Allah be pleased with him, said:

“Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, drew a line for us, then drew other lines around it, to its right and left. He then said: ‘This is Allah’s path, and these are other paths. On each of them is a devil calling people to it.’ Then he recited: ‘And this is My straight path, so follow it, and do not follow other paths, lest they separate you from His path.’”

He, glorified be He, has commanded us to say in our prayers:

“Guide us to the straight path—the path of those upon whom You have bestowed favor, not of those who have incurred Your anger, nor of those who have gone astray.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The Jews have incurred Allah’s anger, and the Christians have gone astray.”

This is because the Jews knew the truth but did not follow it, while the Christians worshipped Allah without knowledge. Hence it used to be said:

“Seek refuge in Allah from the trial posed by the wicked scholar and the ignorant worshipper, for the trial they pose is a trial for all who are put to trial.”

The Most High says:

“When guidance comes to you from Me, whoever follows My guidance will neither go astray nor suffer misery. But whoever turns away from My remembrance will have a life of hardship.”

Ibn ‘Abbās, may Allah be pleased with them both, said: “Allah has guaranteed that whoever reads the Quran and acts upon what it contains will neither go astray in this world nor suffer misery in the Hereafter.” He then recited this verse.

---

## Page 1020

And likewise, the Most High says:

“Alif Lām Mīm.
This is the Book about which there is no doubt, a guidance for the God-fearing,
who believe in the unseen, establish prayer, and spend out of what We have provided for them,
and who believe in what has been revealed to you and what was revealed before you, and are certain of the Hereafter.
Those are upon guidance from their Lord, and those are the successful.”

He thus declares that these people are rightly guided and successful, in contrast to those who have incurred His anger and those who have gone astray. We therefore ask Allah, the Magnificent, to guide us and all our brothers to His straight path—the path of those whom Allah has blessed: the prophets, the utterly truthful, the martyrs, and the righteous. How excellent they are as companions! Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs. All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds. May Allah bless our master Muhammad, his family, and his Companions, and grant them abundant peace.

---

## Page 1021

One of the judges of Wāsit asked Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—to write a creed that he and his household could rely upon.

He replied:

In the name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Especially Merciful.

All praise is due to Allah, who sent His Messenger with guidance and the religion of truth to make it prevail over all religion. Sufficient is Allah as a witness.

I bear witness that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone, with no partner, acknowledging Him and affirming His oneness. And I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and Messenger. May Allah bless him, his family, and his Companions, and grant them ever-abundant peace.

To proceed:

This is the creed of the saved group, supported until the coming of the Hour—the people of the Sunnah and the community. It consists of belief in Allah, His angels, His Books, His messengers, resurrection after death, and the divine decree, both its good and its evil.

Part of belief in Allah is belief in the attributes with which He has described Himself in His Book and those with which He has been described by

---

## Page 1022

His Messenger Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—without distortion or denial, and without specifying how these attributes are or likening them to those of creation. Rather, they believe that Allah—glorified be He—is as He has said:

“There is nothing like unto Him, and He is the Hearing, the Seeing.”

They do not deny Him the attributes with which He has described Himself, distort words from their proper meanings, or deviate from the truth concerning Allah’s names and verses. They neither specify how His attributes are nor liken them to the attributes of His creation. For He—glorified be He—has no peer, no equal, and no rival, nor can He be measured against His creation—glorified and exalted be He. He knows Himself and all others better than His creation does, and His speech is more truthful and more excellent than theirs. His messengers, moreover, are truthful and worthy of belief, unlike those who say about Him what they do not know. Therefore, He—glorified and exalted be He—says:

“Glory be to your Lord, the Lord of Might, far above what they ascribe to Him. Peace be upon the messengers, and all praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds.”

He thus declares Himself far above what those who oppose the messengers ascribe to Him, and pronounces peace upon the messengers because their statements are free from deficiency and fault. In the attributes and names with which He describes Himself, He—glorified be He—combines negation and affirmation. The people of the Sunnah and the community therefore do not depart from what the messengers brought, for it is the straight path: the path of those whom Allah has blessed—the prophets, those steadfast in truth, the martyrs, and the righteous.

Included in this general statement are the attributes with which He describes Himself in Sūrat al-Ikhlās (the Surah of Sincerity), which is equivalent to

---

## Page 1023

one-third of the Quran, where He says:

“Say: He is Allah, the One.”

“Allah, the Eternal Refuge.”

“He neither begets nor is born.”

“Nor is there anyone equal to Him.”

Also included are the attributes with which He describes Himself in the greatest verse of His Book, where He says:

“Allah—there is no deity except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence. Neither drowsiness overtakes Him nor sleep. To Him belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on earth. Who can intercede with Him except by His permission? He knows what is before them and what is behind them, and they encompass nothing of His knowledge except what He wills. His Kursī extends over the heavens and the earth, and preserving them does not weary Him. He is the Most High, the Most Great.”

For this reason, whoever recites this verse at night remains under the protection of a guardian from Allah, and no devil approaches him until morning.

And His saying—glorified be He:

“And put your trust in the Ever-Living, who does not die.”

And His saying—glorified be He:

“He is the First and the Last, the Most High and the Most Near, and He has knowledge of all things.”

And His saying:

“And He is the All-Wise, the All-Aware.”

“He knows what enters the earth and what emerges from it, what descends from heaven and what ascends into it.”

“And with Him are the keys of the unseen; none knows them except Him. He knows whatever is on land and in the sea. Not a leaf falls without His knowing it, nor is there a grain in the darkness of the earth, nor anything moist or dry, except that it is in a clear record.”

And His saying:

“And no female conceives or gives birth except with His knowledge.”

And His saying:

“So that you may know that Allah has power over all things and that Allah encompasses all things in knowledge.”

---

## Page 1030

Section:
On the Sunnah of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace

The Sunnah explains and clarifies the Quran, points to its meaning, and gives expression to it. Likewise, it is obligatory to believe in the descriptions of his Lord, Almighty and Majestic, given by the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in authentic hadiths accepted by knowledgeable scholars.

Among these is his saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Our Lord descends to the lowest heaven every night when the last third of the night remains, and says: ‘Who calls upon Me, that I may answer him? Who asks of Me, that I may give him? Who seeks My forgiveness, that I may forgive him?’”

Reported by al-Bukhārī and Muslim.

And his saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Allah rejoices more in His servant’s repentance than any of you rejoices over his mount.”

The hadith continues. Reported by al-Bukhārī and Muslim.

And his saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Allah laughs at two men: one kills the other, yet both enter Paradise.”

Reported by al-Bukhārī and Muslim.

---

## Page 1031

And his saying:

“Our Lord marvels at His servants’ despair when He is so close to changing their plight. He looks upon you in hardship and despair, and continues to laugh, knowing that your relief is near.”

A good hadith.

And his saying:

“People will continue to be cast into Hell while it says, ‘Are there any more?’ until the Lord of Might places His leg in it—in another narration, His foot upon it—whereupon it contracts upon itself and says, ‘Enough! Enough!’”

Reported by al-Bukhārī and Muslim.

And his saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Allah Most High says, ‘O Adam!’ He replies, ‘At Your service, ever ready to obey.’ Then He calls out in a voice, ‘Allah commands you to send forth from your descendants a contingent to the Fire.’”

Reported by al-Bukhārī and Muslim.

And his saying:

“There is not one of you but that his Lord will speak to him, with no interpreter between them.”

And his saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, concerning a healing supplication for the sick:

“‘Our Lord, Allah, who is in heaven, holy is Your name. Your command is in heaven and on earth. Just as Your mercy is in heaven, place Your mercy on earth. Forgive us our sins and misdeeds. You are the Lord of the righteous. Send down mercy from Your mercy and healing from Your healing upon this affliction.’ Then he will recover.”

A good hadith. Reported by Abū Dāwūd and others.

And his saying:

“Will you not trust me, when I am the trusted one of Him who is in heaven?”

An authentic hadith.

And his saying:

“The Throne is above the water, and Allah is above the Throne, and He knows your condition.”

A good hadith. Reported by Abū Dāwūd and others.

And his saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to the slave woman:

“Where is Allah?”

She replied, “In heaven.”

He asked, “Who am I?”

She replied, “You are the Messenger of Allah.”

He said, “Set her free, for she is a believer.”

Reported by Muslim.

---

## Page 1032

And his saying:

“The best faith is to know that Allah is with you wherever you are.”

A good hadith.

And his saying:

“When one of you stands to pray, he must not spit in front of him or to his right, for Allah is before him. Rather, he should spit to his left or beneath his foot.”

Reported by al-Bukhārī and Muslim.

And his saying:

“O Allah, Lord of the seven heavens and Lord of the mighty Throne, our Lord and the Lord of all things, Splitter of grain and date stones, Revealer of the Torah, the Gospel, and the Quran: I seek refuge in You from the evil of my own soul and from the evil of every creature whose forelock You hold. You are the First, and nothing is before You; You are the Last, and nothing is after You. You are the Most High, and nothing is above You; You are the Inmost, and nothing is nearer than You. Settle my debt for me and free me from poverty.”

Reported by Muslim.

And his saying when his Companions raised their voices in remembrance of Allah:

“O people, be gentle with yourselves. You are not calling upon one who is deaf or absent; you are calling upon One who hears and is near. Indeed, the One you call upon is nearer to each of you than the neck of his mount.”

Reported by al-Bukhārī and Muslim.

And his saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“You will see your Lord just as you see the moon on the night when it is full, without hardship in seeing Him. So if you can avoid being prevented from offering a prayer before sunrise and a prayer before sunset, then do so.”

Reported by al-Bukhārī and Muslim.

And other hadiths like these, in which the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, relates these matters concerning his Lord.

---

## Page 1033

For the saved sect—the adherents of the Sunnah and the community—believe in these matters just as they believe in what Allah has revealed in His mighty Book, without distortion or negation, and without specifying how His attributes are or likening Him to His creation. Rather, they follow the middle course among the sects of this community, just as this community follows the middle course among the nations.

Thus, concerning (the attributes of Allah, glorified and exalted is He, they follow a middle course between the Jahmiyyah, who negate His attributes, and the Mushabbihah, who liken Him to His creation.

Concerning (the acts of Allah Most High, they follow a middle course between the Qadariyyah and the Jabriyyah; and concerning (Allah’s threats of punishment, between the Murji’ah and the Wa‘īdiyyah among the Qadariyyah and others.

Concerning (the designations relating to faith and religion, they follow a middle course between the Harūriyyah and the Mu‘tazilah on one side, and the Murji’ah and the Jahmiyyah on the other; (and concerning the Companions of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, between the Rawāfid and the Khawārij.

---

## Page 1034

Section

Included in what we have mentioned concerning faith in Allah is belief in what Allah has stated in His Book, what has been conveyed from His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, through mass-transmitted reports, and what the early generations of this community unanimously affirmed: that He, glorified is He, is above His heavens, upon His Throne, exalted above His creation; and that He, glorified is He, is with them wherever they may be, knowing what they do. He brings these matters together in His words:

“He is the One who created the heavens and the earth in six days, then established Himself above the Throne. He knows what enters the earth and what emerges from it, what descends from heaven and what ascends into it. And He is with you wherever you are. And Allah sees all that you do.”

His words, “And He is with you,” do not mean that He intermingles with creation. The language does not entail this, and it contradicts both the consensus of the early generations of this community and the innate disposition with which Allah created His creatures. Indeed, the moon—a sign among Allah’s signs and one of the smallest of His creatures—is situated in the sky, yet it is with the traveller and the non-traveller wherever either may be. He, glorified is He, is above the Throne, keeping watch over His creation, overseeing them and fully aware of them, along with the other meanings of His lordship.

All these statements that Allah, glorified is He, has made—that He is above the Throne and that He is with us—are true in their real sense and require no distortion, but must be safeguarded against suppositions

---

## Page 1035

that are false, such as supposing that the apparent meaning of His words, “In heaven,” is that heaven supports Him or covers Him. This is false by the consensus of the people of knowledge and faith. For Allah’s Footstool encompasses the heavens and the earth, and He is the One who keeps the heavens and the earth from passing away and holds back the sky from falling upon the earth except by His permission.

“And among His signs is that the heaven and the earth stand by His command.”

Section

Included in this is belief that He is near to His creation and answers their supplications, as He brings these two matters together in His words:

“And when My servants ask you concerning Me, indeed I am near. I answer the supplication of the supplicant when he calls upon Me.”

To the end of the verse. Also included are the words of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to the Companions when they raised their voices in remembrance of Allah:

“O people, take it easy on yourselves. You are not calling upon one who is deaf or absent. The One upon whom you call is nearer to each of you than the neck of his mount.”

What is mentioned in the Book and the Sunnah concerning His nearness and His being with His creation does not contradict what is mentioned concerning His exaltedness and His being above them. For there is nothing like Him, glorified is He, in any of His attributes. He is exalted in His nearness and near in His exaltedness.

---

## Page 1036

Section

Included in belief in Allah and His Books is belief that the Quran is the speech of Allah, revealed and uncreated. It originated with Him and to Him it returns; Allah Most High truly spoke it, and this Quran, which He sent down to Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is truly Allah’s speech, not the speech of anyone else.

It is not permissible to state without qualification that it is a representation of Allah’s speech or an expression of it. Rather, when people recite it or write it in copies of the Quran, it does not thereby cease to be truly the speech of Allah Most High. For speech is properly attributed to the one who first uttered it, not to the one who conveys and transmits it. It is Allah’s speech, both its letters and its meanings. Allah’s speech is neither the letters without the meanings nor the meanings without the letters.

Section

Also included in what we have mentioned concerning belief in Him, His Books, and His Messengers is belief that the believers will see Him directly with their own eyes on the Day of Resurrection, just as they see the sun in a clear sky with no clouds obscuring it, and just as they see the moon on the night of the full moon, without suffering any difficulty in seeing Him. They will see Him, glorified is He, while they are on the gathering grounds of the Resurrection. Then they will see Him after entering Paradise, as Allah, glorified and exalted is He, wills.

---

## Page 1037

Section

Belief in the Last Day includes believing in everything the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, reported concerning what happens after death. Thus, they believe in the trial of the grave, its punishment, and its bliss. As for the trial, people will be tested in their graves. A person will be asked:

“‘Who is your Lord? What is your religion? Who is your prophet?’ Allah will keep those who believe firm with the firm word, in this worldly life and in the Hereafter. The believer will say, ‘Allah is my Lord, Islam is my religion, and Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is my prophet.’ But the doubter will say, ‘Ah, ah! I do not know. I heard people saying something, so I repeated it.’ He will then be struck with an iron sledgehammer and let out a cry that everything except human beings will hear. If a human being heard it, he would collapse.”

After this trial, there will be either bliss or punishment until the Great Resurrection takes place. Souls will then be restored to their bodies, and the Resurrection will occur—the Resurrection that Allah has foretold in His Book and through His Messenger, and upon which the Muslims are unanimously agreed. People will rise from their graves before the Lord of all worlds, barefoot, naked, and uncircumcised. The sun will draw near to them, and their sweat will reach their mouths. The scales will be set up, and the deeds of Allah’s servants will be weighed in them:

“So those whose scales are heavy—it is they

---

## Page 1038

who will be successful.”

“And those whose scales are light—it is they who have lost themselves, remaining in Hell forever.”

The records—which are the written accounts of deeds—will be laid open. Some will receive their record in their right hand, and others in their left hand or from behind their back, as He, glorified and exalted, says:

“We have bound every person’s deeds to his neck, and on the Day of Resurrection We will bring forth for him a record which he will find spread open.”

“Read your record. Today you yourself are sufficient to take account of yourself.”

Allah will bring His creatures to account and speak privately with His believing servant, making him acknowledge his sins, as described in the Book and the Sunnah. As for the disbelievers, they will not be brought to account in the manner of those whose good and evil deeds are weighed, for they have no good deeds. Rather, their deeds will be counted and enumerated; they will be confronted with them, made to acknowledge them, and recompensed for them.

On the plain of the Resurrection will be the Pool of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to which people will come. Its water is whiter than milk and sweeter than honey. Its vessels are as numerous as the stars in the sky; its length is a month’s journey, and its width is a month’s journey. Whoever takes a drink from it will never thirst again.

The Sirāt will be laid across Hell—it is the bridge between Paradise and Hell. People will cross it according to their deeds: some will pass in the blink of an eye, some like a flash of lightning, some like the wind, and some like a swift steed

---

## Page 1039

and some like riders on camels. Some will run, some will walk, and some will crawl, while others will be snatched away and cast into Hell. For the bridge has hooks that seize people according to their deeds. Whoever crosses the Sirāt will enter Paradise.

Once they have crossed it, they will stop at a bridge between Paradise and Hell, where redress will be exacted from some on behalf of others. When they have been cleansed and purified, they will be permitted to enter Paradise. The first to ask for the gate of Paradise to be opened will be Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and the first community to enter Paradise will be his community.

He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, will have three intercessions on the Day of Resurrection:

The first: his intercession for those gathered for judgment will be accepted, so that judgment may be passed among them, after the prophets—Adam, Noah, Abraham, Moses, and Jesus son of Mary—have each declined to intercede, until it falls to him.

The second: his intercession for the people of Paradise to enter Paradise will be accepted. These two intercessions are exclusive to him.

The third: his intercession for those who deserve the Fire will be accepted. This intercession belongs to him, to the other prophets, to the utterly truthful, and to others. Intercession will be accepted for those who deserve the Fire so that they do not enter it, and for those who have entered it so that they may come out of it. Allah Most High will bring some people out of the Fire without

---

## Page 1040

intercession, but by His grace and mercy. Space will remain in Paradise beyond what is occupied by those who enter it from this world, so Allah will create people for it and admit them to Paradise.

The various matters pertaining to the Hereafter—reckoning, reward, punishment, Paradise and Hell—and their details are described in the books revealed from heaven and in the knowledge transmitted from the prophets. The knowledge inherited from Muhammad concerning these matters is both satisfying and sufficient; whoever seeks it will find it.

The saved group—the people of the Sunnah and the community—believes in divine decree, both its good and its evil. Belief in divine decree has two levels, each comprising two elements.

The first level is to believe that Allah Most High knew all that His creatures would do through His eternal knowledge, which has been His attribute without beginning. He knew all their circumstances: their acts of obedience and disobedience, their provision and their lifespans. Then Allah recorded the decrees concerning His creatures in the Preserved Tablet:

“When Allah first created the Pen, He said to it, ‘Write.’ It said, ‘What shall I write?’ He said, ‘Write all that will happen until the Day of Resurrection.’ Whatever befalls a person could never have missed him, and whatever misses him could never have befallen him. The pens have dried and the scrolls have been folded.”

As He, glorified and exalted, says:

“Do you not know that Allah knows what is in heaven and earth? Indeed, that is in a Record. Indeed, that is easy for Allah.”

He also says:

“There occurs no

---

## Page 1041

calamity on earth or in yourselves except that it is in a Record before We bring it into being. Indeed, that is easy for Allah.”

This determination of decrees—which accords with His knowledge, glorified be He—takes place in various instances, both in general and in detail.
