Other examples include fasting the month of Ramadan, pilgrimage to the Sacred House, and the limits He laid down for them concerning marriage, inheritance, punishments, and sales. They also include the observances He prescribed for them: festivals, Friday prayers, congregational obligatory prayers, and congregational prayers for eclipses, for rain, for funerals, and the Tarāwīh prayers.

They further include what He prescribed for them in everyday customs, such as food, clothing, birth, death, and similar matters: practices, etiquette, and rulings constituting the judgment of Allah and His Messenger among them concerning life, property, sexual relations, honour, benefits, bodily integrity, and other limits and rights, as well as everything else He ordained for them through His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace

---

## Page 1260

and made faith dear to them and beautiful in their hearts. He thus made them followers of His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and protected them from collectively agreeing upon misguidance, as earlier nations had gone astray. Whenever a nation went astray, Allah Most High sent them a messenger, as He says:

“We certainly sent a messenger to every nation, saying, ‘Worship Allah and shun false gods.’”

The Most High also says:

“There was no nation without a warner having come among them.”

Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is the Seal of the Prophets; there is no prophet after him. Allah therefore protected his community from collectively agreeing upon misguidance and placed within it those through whom the proof would be established until the Day of Resurrection. For this reason, their consensus constitutes an authoritative proof, just as the Book and the Sunnah do.

Thus, those within this community who uphold the truth, the Sunnah, and the united body of Muslims are distinguished from the people of falsehood, who claim to follow the Book while turning away from the Sunnah of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and from what the Muslim community has followed. In His Book, Allah commands adherence to His Messenger’s Sunnah and his path. He commands unity and concord and forbids division and disagreement. The Most High says:

“Whoever obeys the Messenger has indeed obeyed Allah.”

The Most High also says:

“We sent no messenger except to be obeyed by Allah’s permission.”

The Most High also says:

“Say, ‘If you love Allah, then follow me; Allah will love you and forgive you your sins.’”

The Most High also says:

“But no, by your Lord, they will not truly believe until they make you judge in their disputes, then find no unease within themselves concerning your judgment and submit wholeheartedly.”

The Most High also says:

“Hold firmly to the rope of Allah, all together, and do not become divided.”

The Most High also says:

---

## Page 1261

“Indeed, those who have divided their religion and become sects—you have nothing to do with them.”

The Most High also says:

“Do not be like those who became divided and differed after clear proofs had come to them.”

“They were commanded only to worship Allah, devoting their religion sincerely to Him, being true in faith, to establish prayer and give zakah. That is the upright religion.”

The Most High also says:

“This is My straight path, so follow it, and do not follow other paths, lest they lead you away from His path.”

The Most High says in the Mother of the Book:

“Guide us to the straight path, the path of those You have blessed, not of those who have incurred Your anger, nor of those who have gone astray.”

It is authentically reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The Jews have incurred Allah’s anger, and the Christians have gone astray.”

Thus, He, glorified be He, commands us in the “Mother of the Book”—whose like was not revealed in the Torah, the Gospel, the Psalms, or the Criterion, which our Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was given from a treasure beneath the Throne, and without which no prayer is valid—to ask Him to guide us to the straight path: the path of those He has blessed, not of those who have incurred His anger, such as the Jews, nor of those who have gone astray, such as the Christians.

This “straight path” is the pure religion of Islam. It is what is contained in the Book of Allah Most High, and it is “the Sunnah and the united body of Muslims.” For the pure Sunnah is the pure religion of Islam. It has been reported from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, through multiple chains of transmission recorded by the compilers of Sunan and Musnad collections, such as Imam Ahmad, Abū Dāwūd, al-Tirmidhī, and others, that he said:

“There will be division within this

---

## Page 1262

community into seventy-two sects, all of which will be in the Fire except one: the united body of Muslims.” In another narration:

“Those who follow the same way that I and my Companions follow today.”

This saved sect is “the people of the Sunnah.” They occupy a middle position among the sects, just as the religion of Islam occupies a middle position among religions. Muslims therefore take a middle course regarding Allah’s prophets, messengers, and righteous servants. They have not gone to extremes concerning them as the Christians did, taking their scholars and monks as lords besides Allah, along with the Messiah, son of Mary, although they were commanded to worship only one God. There is no god but Him; glorified is He above whatever they associate with Him. Nor have they treated them harshly as the Jews did, killing the prophets without right and killing those who called people to justice. Whenever a messenger came to them with something contrary to their desires, they denied some and killed others.

Rather, the believers have believed in Allah’s messengers, honored them, supported them, revered them, loved them, and obeyed them, without worshipping them or taking them as lords. As the Most High says:

“It is not for any human being to whom Allah has given the Scripture, sound judgment, and prophethood to then say to people, ‘Worship me instead of Allah.’ Rather, ‘Be devoted to the Lord through your teaching of the Scripture and your study of it.’”

“Nor would he command you to take the angels and prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have become Muslims?”

Another example is that the believers have taken a middle course concerning “the Messiah”: they have not said that he is Allah or the son of Allah

---

## Page 1263

or the third of three, as the Christians say. Nor have they disbelieved in him and uttered a monstrous slander against Mary, claiming that he was the son of a prostitute, as the Jews alleged. Rather, they have said that he is Allah’s servant and Messenger, His word which He conveyed to Mary, the chaste virgin, and a spirit from Him.

Likewise, the believers “take a middle course concerning the laws of Allah’s religion.” They have not held, as the Jews did, that Allah may not abrogate whatever He wills, or erase and confirm whatever He wills. Allah the Most High relates this about them in His words:

“The foolish among the people will say, ‘What has turned them away from the direction of prayer they used to face?’”

And in His words:

“When they are told, ‘Believe in what Allah has revealed,’ they say, ‘We believe in what was revealed to us,’ and disbelieve in what came after it, although it is the truth, confirming what they have.”

Nor have they permitted their leading scholars and worshippers to change Allah’s religion, commanding whatever they wish and forbidding whatever they wish, as the Christians do. Allah mentions this about them in His words:

“They have taken their scholars and monks as lords besides Allah.”

‘Adī ibn Hātim, may Allah be pleased with him, said: “I said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, surely they did not worship them?’ He replied, ‘They did not worship them; but they made unlawful things lawful for them, and they obeyed them; and they made lawful things unlawful for them, and they obeyed them.’”

The believers have said: “To Allah belong creation and command.” Just as none but Him creates, none but Him commands. They have said, “We hear and obey,” and have obeyed everything Allah has commanded. They have said:

“Indeed, Allah decrees whatever He wills.”

As for a created being, he has no right to alter the command of the Creator, the Most High, however great he may be.

The same applies to the attributes of Allah the Most High: the Jews have described Allah the Most High with the attributes of created beings

---

## Page 1264

, which are imperfect. They said: “He is poor, while we are rich.” They also said: “Allah’s hand is shackled,” and: “He grew tired from creating, so He rested on the Sabbath,” among other such claims.

The Christians, meanwhile, attributed to a created being attributes belonging exclusively to the Creator. They said that he creates and provides, forgives and shows mercy, accepts the repentance of created beings, and rewards and punishes.

The believers have believed in Allah, glorified and exalted is He. He has no peer or rival; none is comparable to Him, and there is nothing like Him. He is the Lord of all worlds and the Creator of everything. All besides Him are His servants, utterly dependent upon Him:

“There is no one in the heavens and earth but that he comes to the Most Merciful as a servant.”

“He has enumerated them and counted them precisely.”

“And each of them will come to Him on the Day of Resurrection alone.”

Another example concerns what is lawful and unlawful. Regarding the Jews, Allah the Most High says:

“For wrongdoing on the part of the Jews, We made unlawful for them good things which had been lawful to them.”

Thus, they do not eat animals with claws, such as camels and ducks, nor the fat covering the stomach and intestines or surrounding the kidneys, nor a young goat in its mother’s milk. These are among the foods, clothing, and other things forbidden to them. It has even been said that three hundred and sixty categories of things were forbidden to them, while two hundred and forty-eight commandments were obligatory upon them. Likewise, stringent restrictions concerning impurities were imposed upon them, to the point that they would neither eat with a menstruating woman nor share a house with her.

The Christians, by contrast, deemed foul things and all forbidden things lawful, and came into contact with every kind of impurity. Yet the Messiah had only said to them:

“And to make lawful for you some of what was forbidden to you.”

---

## Page 1265

And for this reason, the Most High says:

“Fight those who do not believe in Allah or in the Last Day, who do not consider unlawful what Allah and His Messenger have made unlawful, and who do not follow the religion of truth, among those who were given the Scripture, until they pay the jizyah willingly while they are humbled.”

As for the believers, they are as Allah describes them in His words:

“My mercy encompasses all things. So I will decree it for those who fear Me and give zakah, and those who believe in Our signs.”

“Those who follow the Messenger, the unlettered Prophet, whom they find written about in the Torah and the Gospel that are with them. He enjoins upon them what is right and forbids them what is wrong, makes lawful for them good things and forbids them foul things, and relieves them of their burden and the shackles that were upon them. So those who believe in him, honour him, support him, and follow the light sent down with him—it is they who are successful.”

This is a subject that would require a lengthy discussion. Likewise, the people of the Sunnah and the community occupy a middle position among the sects. In “matters concerning Allah’s names, signs, and attributes,” they stand between “those who deny His attributes,” who deviate concerning Allah’s names and signs and deny the reality of the attributes with which Allah has described Himself, to the point of likening Him to nonexistence and lifeless things; and “those who liken Him to creation,” who draw comparisons for Him and liken Him to created beings.

The people of the Sunnah and the community therefore believe in the attributes with which Allah has described Himself and with which His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, has described Him, without distortion or denial, and without describing how those attributes are or likening them to those of created beings.

In “matters concerning His creation and command,” they stand between those who deny Allah’s power, who

---

## Page 1266

do not believe in His perfect power, His all-encompassing will, or His creation of everything; and those who corrupt Allah’s religion by holding that the servant has neither will, nor power, nor action. They thus nullify commands and prohibitions, reward and punishment, becoming like the polytheists who said:

“If Allah had willed, we would not have associated partners with Him, nor would our fathers, nor would we have forbidden anything.”

The people of the Sunnah believe that Allah has power over all things: He is able to guide His servants and turn their hearts. Whatever Allah wills comes to pass, and whatever He does not will does not come to pass. Nothing occurs in His dominion that He does not will, nor is He unable to secure what He wills. He is the Creator of everything—entities, attributes, and movements.

They also believe that the servant has power, will, and action, and that he acts by choice. They do not describe him as compelled, for someone who is compelled is forced to act against his choice. Allah, glorified is He, has made the servant choose what he does: he chooses and wills, while Allah is his Creator and the Creator of his choice. There is no parallel to this, for nothing is like Allah, whether in His essence, His attributes, or His actions.

In “matters concerning designations and rulings, promises and threats,” they occupy a middle position between the Wa'īdiyyah, who hold that Muslims who commit major sins will remain in the Fire forever, exclude them entirely from faith, and deny the intercession of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace; and the Murji'ah, who say: “The faith of sinful people is like the faith of the prophets, and righteous deeds are not part of religion or faith.” They deny threats of punishment and punishment altogether.

---

## Page 1267

The people of the Sunnah and the Community therefore believe that sinful Muslims retain some faith and its foundation, but do not possess all the obligatory faith that would entitle them to Paradise. They believe that such Muslims will not remain in the Fire forever. Rather, whoever has a grain’s weight or a mustard seed’s weight of faith in his heart will leave it, and the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, has reserved his intercession for those of his community who commit major sins.

Regarding “the Companions of the Messenger of Allah,” may Allah bless him and grant him peace and be pleased with them, they likewise occupy a middle position between two extremes. On one side are those who go to excess concerning ʿAlī, may Allah be pleased with him: they rank him above Abū Bakr and ʿUmar, may Allah be pleased with them both, and believe that he alone, unlike those two, is the infallible imam. They believe that the Companions committed injustice and grave sins, and likewise declare the community after them to be unbelievers. Some even regard him as a prophet or a god. On the other side are those who are hostile to him: they consider him and ʿUthmān, may Allah be pleased with them both, unbelievers, and deem it lawful to shed their blood and that of anyone who supports them. They consider it commendable to revile ʿAlī, ʿUthmān, and others like them, and impugn ʿAlī’s caliphate and leadership, may Allah be pleased with him.

Likewise, in all other “matters of the Sunnah,” they occupy a middle position, because they hold firmly to the Book of Allah, the Sunnah of His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and what was agreed upon by the earliest forerunners among the Emigrants and the Helpers and those who followed them in righteousness.

---

## Page 1268

Section

As for you—may Allah set you right—Allah has blessed you with belonging to Islam, which is His religion, and has spared you the afflictions of those outside Islam, among the polytheists and the People of the Book. Islam is the greatest and most exalted of blessings, for Allah accepts no other religion from anyone:

“Whoever seeks a religion other than Islam, it will never be accepted from him, and in the Hereafter he will be among the losers.”

Through your adherence to the Sunnah, Allah has also spared you most misleading innovations, including many of the innovations of the Rāfidah, Jahmiyyah, Kharijites, and Qadariyyah. He has instilled in you a hatred for those who deny Allah’s names and attributes, His decree and predestination, or revile the Companions of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—a hatred consistent with the way of the people of the Sunnah and the Community. This is among Allah’s greatest blessings upon those whom He has favoured with it, for it is part of the fullness of faith and the perfection of religion.

For this reason, righteous and devout people, as well as warriors who strive in Allah’s cause, have been so numerous among you that their like is not found among the groups of religious innovators. Within the victorious Muslim armies and the divinely supported troops of Allah, there have always been men from among you through whom Allah strengthens the religion and brings honour to the believers. Among your ascetics and worshippers are those who possess pure spiritual states, follow a pleasing path, and are granted spiritual disclosures and extraordinary feats.

---

## Page 1269

Among you, too, are pious friends of Allah who enjoy an honourable reputation among mankind. For the early shaykhs among you, such as the one entitled Shaykh al-Islām, “Abū al-Hasan Alī ibn Ahmad ibn Yūsuf al-Qurashī al-Hakkārī,” and, after him, the shaykh of spiritual insight and exemplary conduct, Adī ibn Musāfir al-Umawī, “and those who followed their path possessed such virtue, religious devotion, righteousness, and adherence to the Sunnah that Allah exalted their standing and raised their renown. Shaykh Adī”—may Allah sanctify his soul—was among the most virtuous of Allah’s righteous servants and the foremost shaykhs whose example was followed. He possessed pure spiritual states and lofty merits known to those acquainted with such matters. He is widely renowned throughout the Muslim community and remembered with honour.

The creed preserved from him did not depart from that of the earlier shaykhs whose path he followed, such as the righteous shaykh and imam, “Abū al-Faraj Abd al-Wāhid ibn Muhammad ibn Alī al-Ansārī al-Shīrāzī, later” al-Dimashqī, “and likewise Shaykh al-Islām al-Hakkārī,” and others like them.

In the major doctrinal principles, these shaykhs did not depart from the principles of “the people of the Sunnah and the Community.” Rather, their encouragement of these principles, their call to uphold them, their eagerness to spread them, and their open opposition to those who contradicted them—together with their religious devotion, virtue, and righteousness—were such that Allah exalted their standing and raised their renown. Most of what they say concerning these major principles is sound, although their statements and those of their peers inevitably include some views on particular questions

---

## Page 1270

that are less well-founded, as well as weak arguments, such as hadiths that are not reliably established and analogies that do not consistently hold. This is to be considered alongside what people of insight know: everyone’s statements may be accepted or rejected except those of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. This applies especially to later generations of the Muslim community who have not attained a firm knowledge and understanding of the Book and the Sunnah, nor learned to distinguish sound hadiths from unsound ones and analogies that yield valid conclusions from those that do not. Added to this are the dominance of personal desires, the proliferation of opinions, the intensification of disagreement and division, and the emergence of hostility and discord.

These and similar causes intensify the “ignorance and injustice” with which Allah described man when He said:

“Yet man undertook it. Indeed, he was most unjust and ignorant.”

When Allah blesses a person with knowledge and justice, He rescues him from this misguidance. He, glorified be He, has said:

“By time, indeed, mankind is in loss, except those who believe and do righteous deeds, and counsel one another to uphold the truth and counsel one another to be patient.”

And He, Most High, has said:

“And We made from among them leaders guiding by Our command when they were patient and firmly believed in Our signs.”

You know—may Allah set your affairs aright—that the “Sunnah” which must be followed, whose adherents are praised and whose opponents are censured, is the Sunnah of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in matters of belief, worship, and all other religious matters. It can only be known through knowledge of the reliably established hadiths of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, concerning his words and deeds, and the words and deeds from which he refrained; then through knowledge of the way followed by the earliest believers and those who followed them in righteousness.

---

## Page 1271

And this is found in “the well-known compilations of Islam,” such as the two Sahīh collections of al-Bukhārī and Muslim, and the Sunan collections, including the Sunan of Abū Dāwūd and al-Nasā’ī, the Jāmi‘ of al-Tirmidhī, and the Muwatta’ of Imam Mālik, as well as the well-known collections arranged by narrator, such as the Musnad of Imam Ahmad and others.

The books of “Quranic exegesis,” “the Prophet’s military campaigns,” and all other “hadith collections,” in their entirety and in their individual parts, contain reports, some of which provide evidence for others. Allah has raised up knowledgeable scholars who have devoted themselves to this field, through whose efforts He has preserved the religion for its followers.

Groups of scholars have compiled the hadiths and reports transmitted concerning “the beliefs of the people of the Sunnah,” including Hammād ibn Salama, Abd al-Rahmān ibn Mahdī, Abd Allāh ibn Abd al-Rahmān al-Dārimī, Uthmān ibn Sa‘īd al-Dārimī, and others of their generation. Similarly, al-Bukhārī, Abū Dāwūd, al-Nasā’ī, Ibn Mājah, and others have devoted chapters to these subjects in their books. The same is true of the works of Abū Bakr al-Athram, Abd Allāh ibn Ahmad, Abū Bakr al-Khallāl, Abū al-Qāsim al-Tabarānī, Abū al-Shaykh al-Asbahānī, Abū Bakr al-Ājurrī, Abū al-Hasan al-Dāraqutnī, Abū Abd Allāh ibn Mandah, Abū al-Qāsim al-Lālakā’ī, Abū Abd Allāh Ibn Batta, Abū Amr al-Talamankī, Abū Nu‘aym al-Asbahānī, Abū Bakr al-Bayhaqī, and Abū Dharr al-Harawī—although some of these works contain weak hadiths that those with expertise can recognize.

Many people may also narrate, concerning the divine attributes and all other matters of belief

---

## Page 1272

and religious matters generally, numerous hadiths falsely fabricated and attributed to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. These fall into two categories:

The first consists of false statements that it is impermissible to utter, let alone attribute to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

The second consists of statements made by some of the early generations, scholars, or other people, which may be true, concern matters open to scholarly judgment, or represent the views of those who expressed them, but are then attributed to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. This is common among those unfamiliar with hadith. An example is the set of questions compiled by Shaykh “Abū al-Faraj Abd al-Wāhid ibn Muhammad ibn Alī al-Ansārī,” which he made a test for distinguishing a follower of the Sunnah from an innovator. These are “well-known questions” concocted by fabricators, who supplied a chain of transmission leading back to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and presented them as his words. Anyone with even the slightest knowledge recognizes this as a fabrication and a false attribution.

Although most of these questions accord with the fundamental principles of the Sunnah, some concern matters on which a person who disagrees is not to be judged an innovator. One example is the question of the first blessing He bestowed upon His servant. The people of the Sunnah disagree over this question, but their disagreement is merely verbal, since it turns on whether pleasure followed by pain is to be called a blessing. These questions also contain some less well-founded views.

It is therefore obligatory to distinguish authentic hadiths from fabricated ones, for the Sunnah is truth, not falsehood; it consists of authentic hadiths, not fabricated ones. This is “a fundamental principle of great importance” for Muslims generally, and especially for those who claim adherence to the Sunnah.

---

## Page 1273

Section

It has already been explained that Allah’s religion takes a middle course between those who go to excess in it and those who fall short of it.

Whenever Allah Most High commands His servants to do something, Satan intervenes in one of two ways, caring little which succeeds: either excess or neglect.

If Satan has obstructed many who profess Islam—the religion of Allah, other than which no religion is accepted from anyone—to the point of drawing them away from many of its ordinances, he has even drawn some of the most devout and scrupulous groups of this community out of it, so that they passed out of it as an arrow passes through its quarry. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, commanded that those who pass out of it be fought. It is authentically reported from him in the collections of authentic hadith and elsewhere, through the Commander of the Believers, “Alī ibn Abī Tālib, Abū Saīd al-Khudrī, Sahl ibn Hunayf, Abū Dharr al-Ghifārī, Sa‘d ibn Abī Waqqās, Abd Allāh ibn Umar, and Ibn Masūd”—may Allah be pleased with them—and others, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, mentioned the Khawārij and said:

“One of you would consider his prayer insignificant beside their prayer, his fasting beside their fasting, and his recitation beside their recitation. They recite the Quran, but it does not go beyond their throats. They pass out of Islam as an arrow passes through its quarry. Wherever you encounter them, kill them—or fight them—for whoever kills them will have a reward with Allah on the Day of Resurrection. If I live to encounter them, I will surely slay them as ‘Ād were slain.”

In another narration: “The worst of those slain beneath the canopy of heaven; the best

---

## Page 1274

of those slain are those whom they kill.” In another narration:

“If those who fight them knew what reward has been reserved for them, as conveyed by Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, they would give up further deeds.”

When these people rebelled during the caliphate of the Commander of the Believers, ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib—may Allah be pleased with him—he and the Companions of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, fought them in accordance with the Prophet’s command and his exhortation to fight them. All the leading scholars of Islam agreed that they should be fought. The same applies to all those who separate from the Muslim community and depart from the Sunnah and Law of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, following misleading desires and contrary innovations.

For this reason, the Muslims also fought the “Rāfidah,” who are worse than these people. They declare the vast majority of Muslims to be disbelievers, including the first three caliphs and others. They claim that they alone are believers and that everyone else is a disbeliever. They declare anyone who says that Allah will be seen in the Hereafter, or who believes in Allah’s attributes, His perfect power, and His all-encompassing will, to be a disbeliever. They likewise declare anyone who disagrees with the innovations they follow to be a disbeliever.

They wipe their feet but do not wipe over leather socks; they delay breaking the fast and performing the prayer until a star appears; they combine two prayers without an excuse; they recite qunūt supplications in all five prayers; and they prohibit fuqqā‘, animals slaughtered by the People of the Book, and animals slaughtered by Muslims who disagree with them, because they regard those Muslims as disbelievers. And they speak against the Companions—may

---

## Page 1275

Allah be pleased with them—in grievous terms that need not be mentioned here, besides other things. Thus, the Muslims fought them at the command of Allah and His Messenger.

If, during the time of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his rightly guided caliphs, there were people who professed Islam yet departed from it despite their great devotion to worship—so much so that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, commanded that they be fought—then it follows that someone who professes Islam or adherence to the Sunnah in these times may likewise depart from Islam and the Sunnah. Someone may even claim to follow the Sunnah while not being one of its adherents, but having departed from it. This happens for various “reasons”:

One is the excess that Allah Most High condemned in His Book, saying:

“O People of the Book, do not exceed the bounds in your religion, and say nothing about Allah except the truth. The Messiah, Jesus, son of Mary, was only a messenger of Allah, and His word which He conveyed to Mary, and a spirit from Him,”

continuing to His words:

“And Allah is sufficient as a Guardian.”

Allah Most High also said:

“Say, ‘O People of the Book, do not exceed the bounds in your religion beyond the truth, and do not follow the desires of people who went astray before, led many astray, and strayed from the right path.’”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Beware of excess in religion, for it was excess in religion that destroyed those before you.”

This is an authentic hadith.

Another cause is division and disagreement, which Allah Most High mentioned in His mighty Book.

Another is reports narrated from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, which those knowledgeable in hadith unanimously agree are falsely attributed to him. A person ignorant of hadith hears them and believes them because they accord with his assumptions and desires.

---

## Page 1276

“The worst form of misguidance” is following conjecture and desire, as Allah Most High said concerning those whom He condemned:

“They follow nothing but conjecture and what their souls desire, although guidance has already come to them from their Lord.”

He said concerning His Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“By the star when it descends,”

“Your companion has neither gone astray nor erred,”

“Nor does he speak from his own desire,”

“It is nothing but revelation sent down to him.”

Thus, He declared him free of misguidance and error, which are ignorance and wrongdoing: the misguided person does not know the truth, while the erring person follows his desires. He also stated that he does not speak from personal desire; rather, it is revelation that Allah has revealed to him. He therefore affirmed his knowledge and declared him free of following desire.

I shall set out the main false principles introduced as innovations by groups among those who profess adherence to the Sunnah, yet have broken away from it and become some of the greatest wrongdoers.

These are arranged in chapters:

---

## Page 1277

Chapter One:

Reports they have narrated concerning the divine attributes, beyond those found in the Islamic hadith collections, which we know with absolute certainty to be lies and slander—indeed, heinous disbelief. They may also utter statements of disbelief for which they narrate no hadith.

One example is a report they narrate:

“Allah descends towards evening on the day of ‘Arafah upon a dark-grey camel, shaking hands with those who are riding and embracing those on foot.”

This is among the greatest lies against Allah and His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Whoever says it is among the worst of those who speak falsely about Allah. No Muslim scholar has ever narrated this report; rather, Muslim scholars and hadith experts unanimously agree that it is falsely attributed to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Scholars such as Ibn Qutaybah and others have said that this report and others like it were fabricated by unbelieving heretics to discredit the scholars of hadith by claiming that they narrate such things.

Likewise, another report states:

“When he departed from Muzdalifah, he saw his Lord walking ahead of the pilgrims, wearing a woollen robe.”

Or something resembling this slander and fabrication against Allah, which no one who knows Allah and His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would utter.

---

## Page 1278

Likewise, there is a report stating:

“Allah walks upon the earth, and whenever there is a patch of greenery, they say: ‘This is where His feet were.’”

They recite His words, Most High:

“So observe the effects of Allah’s mercy—how He gives life to the earth after its death.”

This too is a lie, by the unanimous agreement of the scholars. Allah did not say, “Observe the traces of Allah’s footsteps.” Rather, He said, “the effects of Allah’s mercy”; His mercy here means the vegetation.

Likewise, some reports state that Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, “saw his Lord while circumambulating the Kaaba”; others state that “he saw Him while leaving Mecca”; and others that “he saw Him in one of the lanes of Medina,” along with other such accounts. Every report stating that “Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw his Lord with his own eyes on earth” is a lie, by the unanimous agreement of Muslims and their scholars. No Muslim scholar has ever said or narrated such a thing.

The disagreement among the Companions concerned only whether Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw his Lord on the night of the Ascension. Ibn ‘Abbās, may Allah be pleased with them both, and most scholars of the Sunnah held that Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw his Lord on the night of the Ascension. ‘Ā’ishah, may Allah be pleased with her, and a group who shared her view denied this. ‘Ā’ishah, may Allah be pleased with her, neither narrated anything from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, on this matter nor asked him about it.

Nor has anything concerning this been transmitted from al-Siddīq, may Allah be pleased with him, contrary to what some ignorant people relate:

“Her father asked the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and he answered, ‘Yes,’ whereas he told ‘Ā’ishah, ‘No.’”

This report is a lie, by the unanimous agreement of the scholars. For this reason, Judge Abū Ya‘lā and others mentioned that differing reports have been transmitted from Imam

---

## Page 1279

Ahmad—may Allah have mercy on him—on whether one should say that Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw his Lord with his own eyes; or that he saw Him with the eye of his heart; or simply that he saw Him, without specifying either his own eyes or the eye of his heart. Three positions have been transmitted from him.

Likewise, the hadith transmitted by the scholars in which he said:

“I saw my Lord in such-and-such a form,”

is narrated through Ibn ‘Abbās, Umm al-Tufayl, and others. It includes the words:

“He placed His hand between my shoulders until I felt the coolness of His fingertips upon my chest.”

This did not occur on the night of the Ascension; rather, the event related in this hadith occurred in Medina. The hadith states:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, slept through the dawn prayer, then came out to them and said, ‘I saw such-and-such.’”

It is narrated by those who prayed behind him only in Medina, such as Umm al-Tufayl and others. The Ascension, however, began in Mecca, by the unanimous agreement of the scholars and according to the explicit text of the Quran and the mass-transmitted Sunnah. As Allah Most High says:

“Glory be to Him who took His servant by night from the Sacred Mosque to the Farthest Mosque.”

It is therefore clear that this hadith describes a dream seen in Medina, as many of its narrations explicitly explain: “It was a dream.” Although the dreams of prophets are revelation, this was not a waking vision on the night of the Ascension.

The Muslims have unanimously agreed that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, did not see his Lord with his own eyes on earth, and that Allah did not descend to earth for him. No hadith has ever been transmitted from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stating that “Allah descended to earth for him.” Rather, the authentic hadiths state that “Allah draws near on the evening of ‘Arafah”; and in another narration, “to the lowest heaven every night, when there remains

---

## Page 1280

the last third of the night, and He says, ‘Who calls upon Me, that I may answer him? Who asks of Me, that I may give him? Who seeks My forgiveness, that I may forgive him?’”

It is also established in the Sahīh that “Allah draws near on the evening of ‘Arafah”; and in another narration, “to the lowest heaven, and He speaks proudly of the people of ‘Arafah to the angels, saying, ‘Look at My servants: they have come to Me dishevelled and dusty. What do these people seek?’”

It has also been narrated that “Allah descends on the night of mid-Sha‘bān”—if the hadith is authentic, for its authenticity has been questioned by the scholars.

Likewise, the report narrated by some that “when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, came down from Hirā’, his Lord appeared to him upon a chair between heaven and earth” is erroneous by the agreement of the scholars. Rather, the authentic collections relate that the one who appeared to him was the angel who had come to him at Hirā’ the first time and said to him, “Read!” The Prophet related:

“I replied, ‘I cannot read.’ He took hold of me and pressed me until I could bear no more. Then he released me and said, ‘Read!’ I replied, ‘I cannot read.’ He took hold of me and pressed me until I could bear no more. Then he released me and said, ‘Read in the name of your Lord who created—created man from a clinging clot. Read! And your Lord is the Most Generous, who taught by the pen—taught man what he did not know.’”

This was the first revelation sent down to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, then went on to describe the interruption in revelation. He said:

“As I was walking, I heard a voice. I raised my head, and there was the angel who had come to me at Hirā’, sitting upon a chair between heaven and earth.”

This was narrated by Jābir, may Allah be pleased with him, in the two Sahīh collections. Thus, he stated that he saw the angel who had come to him at Hirā’ between heaven and earth, and he mentioned that he was terrified

---

## Page 1281

of him. In some narrations, the word al-malak (the angel) appeared, but the reader mistook it for al-Malik (the King), supposing that it referred to Allah. This is erroneous and false.

In short, every hadith stating that “the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw his Lord with his own eyes on earth,” that “He descended to earth for him,” that “the gardens of Paradise arise from the footsteps of the True One,” or that “He stepped upon the rock in Jerusalem”—all such reports are false fabrications, by the agreement of Muslim scholars, both hadith specialists and others.

Likewise, anyone who claims to have seen his Lord with his own eyes before death makes a false claim, by the agreement of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā‘ah. They unanimously agree that no believer will see his Lord with his physical eyes until he dies. This is established in Sahīh Muslim, from al-Nawwās ibn Sam‘ān, who reported that when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, mentioned the Dajjāl, he said:

“Know that none of you will see his Lord until he dies.”

This has also been narrated from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, through other chains of transmission. He warned his community against the trial of the Dajjāl and explained to them that “none of them will see his Lord until he dies,” so that no one should suppose that the Dajjāl he has seen is his Lord.

However, what those who have realized the truths of faith experience—knowledge of Allah, certainty in their hearts, and the witnessing and manifestations experienced by those hearts—has many degrees. When Gabriel, peace be upon him, asked the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, about excellence in worship, he said:

“Excellence in worship is to worship Allah as though you see Him; and though you do not see Him, He sees you.”

---

## Page 1282

A believer may see his Lord in a dream in various forms, according to the degree of his faith and certainty. If his faith is sound, he sees Him only in a beautiful form; if his faith is deficient, he sees what corresponds to his faith. A dream vision is to be understood differently from an actual vision while awake. It calls for “interpretation and explanation” because it contains symbolic representations of realities.

Some people may also experience visions while awake similar to those experienced by someone asleep: they see with their hearts something like what a sleeping person sees. Certain realities may also become manifest to a person, who witnesses them with his heart. All of this occurs in this world.

Sometimes a person is so overwhelmed by what his heart witnesses and his senses take in that he thinks he has seen it with his physical eyes, until he awakens and realizes that it was a dream. At other times, he knows even while dreaming that it is a dream. Likewise, some devout worshippers experience an inward witnessing that so overwhelms them that they lose awareness of their senses. They then mistake it for seeing with their eyes. They are mistaken in this. Any devout worshipper, whether of earlier or later times, who says that he has seen his Lord with his physical eyes is mistaken, by the consensus of the people of knowledge and faith.

Indeed, believers will see Allah with their eyes in Paradise, and people will also see Him on the plains of the Resurrection, as established in the mass-transmitted hadiths from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“You will surely see your Lord as you see the sun at midday with no clouds obscuring it, and as you see the full moon on a clear night with no clouds obscuring it.”

---

## Page 1283

And he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“There are four gardens of Firdaws: two gardens whose vessels, ornaments, and everything within them are made of gold, and two gardens whose vessels, ornaments, and everything within them are made of silver. Nothing stands between the people and their looking upon their Lord except the cloak of majesty upon His Face in the Garden of Eden.”

And he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When the people of Paradise enter Paradise, a caller will announce: ‘O people of Paradise, Allah has made you a promise that He wishes to fulfil for you.’ They will say: ‘What is it? Has He not brightened our faces, made our scales heavy, admitted us to Paradise, and protected us from the Fire?’ Then He will remove the veil, and they will look upon Him. He will have given them nothing dearer to them than looking upon Him. This is the additional reward.”

These and other hadiths are found in the authentic collections. The early generations and the leading scholars accepted them, and the people of the Sunnah and the community are agreed upon them. Only the Jahmiyyah and their followers among the Muʿtazilah, the Rāfidah, and others like them deny or distort them: those who deny Allah’s attributes, seeing Him, and other such matters. They are the deniers of the divine attributes, the worst of all created beings. Allah’s religion takes a middle course between these people’s denial of what His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, reported concerning the Hereafter, and the extremists’ belief that He can be seen with physical eyes in this world. Both positions are false.

Those who claim to see Him with their physical eyes in this world are misguided, as explained above. If they add to this the claim that they see Him in particular persons—whether righteous people, beardless youths, kings, or others—their misguidance becomes all the greater

---

## Page 1284

and their disbelief likewise. They are then more astray than the Christians who claim to have seen Him in the form of Jesus son of Mary.

Indeed, they are more astray than the followers of the Dajjāl, who will appear at the end of time and tell people, “I am your Lord.” He will command the sky to rain and the earth to bring forth vegetation, and will say to a ruined place, “Bring out your treasures,” whereupon its treasures will follow him. This is the one against whom the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, warned his community. He said:

“From the creation of Adam until the Hour is established, there is no trial greater than the Dajjāl.”

He also said:

“When any of you sits during prayer, let him seek refuge in Allah from four things, saying: ‘O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the punishment of Hell; I seek refuge in You from the punishment of the grave; I seek refuge in You from the trials of life and death; and I seek refuge in You from the trial of the false messiah, the Dajjāl.’”

This one claims lordship and produces deceptive signs by which he leads people astray, such that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said of him:

“He is blind in one eye, whereas your Lord is not blind in one eye. Know that none of you will see his Lord until he dies.”

He thus gave them two clear signs that everyone can recognize, knowing, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that some people would go astray and consider it possible to see their Lord in this world in human form—like these misguided people who hold this belief. Such people are sometimes called the “Hulūliyyah” (advocates of divine indwelling) and the “Ittihādiyyah” (advocates of union).

They fall into two groups:

One group restricts His indwelling or union to certain things, as the Christians maintain

---

## Page 1285

concerning the Messiah, peace be upon him, and as the extremists do concerning 'Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, and others like him. Some hold this concerning various shaykhs, others concerning certain kings, and others concerning certain beautiful forms, along with other doctrines worse than the Christians’ teaching.

Another “group” makes this universal, affirming His indwelling or union in all existing things—even dogs, pigs, impure substances, and the like. This is maintained by some of the Jahmiyyah and their followers among the advocates of union, such as the followers of Ibn 'Arabī, Ibn Sab'īn, Ibn al-Fārid, al-Tilmisānī, al-Balyānī, and others.

The doctrine of all the messengers and their followers among the believers and the People of the Scriptures is that Allah, glorified be He, is the Creator of all worlds, the Lord of the heavens, the earth, and everything between them, and the Lord of the mighty Throne. All created beings are His servants and stand in need of Him. He, glorified be He, is above His heavens, over His Throne, distinct from His creation. Yet He is with them wherever they are, as He, glorified and exalted be He, says:

“He is the One who created the heavens and the earth in six days, then rose over the Throne. He knows what enters the earth and what emerges from it, what descends from heaven and what ascends into it. He is with you wherever you are, and Allah sees all that you do.”

These “misguided disbelievers” claim to see their Lord with their own eyes. One of them may even claim to have sat with Him and conversed with Him, or lain beside Him. Sometimes one of them identifies a human being—whether a particular individual;

---

## Page 1286

or a boy, or someone else—and claims that He spoke to them. Such people are to be called upon to repent. If they repent, well and good; otherwise, they are to be beheaded. They are disbelievers, indeed more deeply disbelieving than the Jews and Christians

“who said that Allah is the Messiah, son of Mary.”

For the Messiah is a noble messenger, honoured by Allah in this world and the Hereafter, and among those brought near to Him. If Allah has declared those who said that the Messiah is Allah, or that Allah united with him or dwelt within him, to be disbelievers, and their disbelief is so grave—indeed, even those who said that He had taken a son, concerning whom He said:

“And they say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken a son.’”
“You have certainly made an atrocious claim.”
“The heavens almost rupture at it, the earth splits open, and the mountains collapse in ruin,”
“because they attribute a son to the Most Merciful.”
“It is not fitting for the Most Merciful to take a son.”
“There is none in the heavens and the earth but will come to the Most Merciful as a servant.”

—then what of someone who claims that a particular person is Allah Himself? Such a person is more deeply disbelieving than the extremists who claim that ‘Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, or another member of the Prophet’s household, is Allah.

These are the “heretics” whom ‘Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, burned with fire. He ordered trenches to be dug for them at the Gate of Kindah and cast them into them after giving them three days to repent. When they did not repent, he burned them with fire. The Companions, may Allah be pleased with them, agreed that they should be put to death. However, Ibn ‘Abbās, may Allah be pleased with him and his father, held that they should be killed by the sword without being burned. This is the position of most scholars, and their story is well known among scholars.

---

## Page 1287

Section

The same applies to excessive veneration of certain shaykhs, whether Shaykh “‘Adī,” Yūnus al-Qatī, al-Hallāj, or others; indeed, it applies to excessive veneration of ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib, may Allah be pleased with him, and others like him—even of the Messiah, peace be upon him, and others like him.

If anyone excessively venerates a living person, or a righteous man such as ‘Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, “‘Adī,” or someone like him, or someone believed to be righteous, such as al-Hallāj, al-Hākim who was in Egypt, Yūnus al-Qatī, or others like them, and attributes some form of divinity to him—for example, by saying, “I want no provision that Shaykh so-and-so does not grant me”; saying, when slaughtering a sheep, “In the name of my master”; worshipping him by prostrating to him or to someone else; or calling upon him instead of Allah Most High, saying, for example, “O my master so-and-so, forgive me,” or “Have mercy on me,” or “Grant me victory,” or “Provide for me,” or “Come to my aid,” or “Grant me protection,” or “I put my trust in you,” or “You are sufficient for me,” or “I am in your care”—then all this, and similar words and deeds involving the exclusive prerogatives of lordship that belong to Allah Most High alone, constitutes polytheism and misguidance.

Such a person is to be called upon to repent. If he repents, well and good; otherwise, he is to be put to death. For Allah sent the messengers and revealed the Scriptures only so that we would worship Allah alone, without any partner, and not set up another god alongside Him.

---

## Page 1288

And those who used to invoke other gods alongside Allah—such as the sun, the moon, the stars, Ezra, the Messiah, the angels, al-Lāt, al-‘Uzzā, Manāt, the third, the other one, Yaghūth, Ya‘ūq, Nasr, or others—did not believe that they created living beings, sent down rain, or caused plants to grow. Rather, they worshipped the prophets, angels, stars, jinn, and statues representing them, or worshipped their graves, saying, “We worship them only so that they may bring us nearer to Allah.” They also said, “They are our intercessors with Allah.” So Allah sent His messengers to forbid invoking anyone besides Him, whether in worship or in appeals for deliverance.

Allah Most High said:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Him. They have no power to remove harm from you or to divert it.’”

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, seeking to be closest to Him. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is to be feared.”

Some of the early generations said that there were people who invoked the Messiah, Ezra, and the angels. Allah therefore said to them: “Those whom you invoke draw near to Me just as you draw near, hope for My mercy just as you hope for My mercy, and fear My punishment just as you fear My punishment.”

Allah Most High also said:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Allah. They possess not even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth. They have no share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.’”

“Intercession before Him benefits no one except those for whom He has given permission.”

Thus He, glorified be He, declared that whatever is invoked besides Allah does not possess even an atom’s weight

---

## Page 1289

in His dominion, nor any share in it; that He has no helper among His creation whose assistance He needs; and that intercession before Him is of no benefit except by His permission.

Allah Most High said:

“How many angels there are in the heavens whose intercession avails nothing, except after Allah gives permission for whom He wills and approves.”

Allah Most High also said:

“Have they taken intercessors besides Allah? Say, ‘Even though they possess nothing and have no understanding?’”

“Say, ‘All intercession belongs to Allah. His is the dominion of the heavens and the earth. Then to Him you will be returned.’”

Allah Most High also said:

“They worship besides Allah what can neither harm nor benefit them, and they say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Are you informing Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on earth?’”

And the verse continues.

Worshipping Allah alone is the foundation of the religion. This is the monotheism with which Allah sent the messengers and revealed the Scriptures. Allah Most High said:

“Ask those of Our messengers whom We sent before you: ‘Did We appoint any gods besides the Most Merciful to be worshipped?’”

Allah Most High also said:

“We certainly sent a messenger to every nation, saying, ‘Worship Allah and shun false objects of worship.’”

Allah Most High also said:

“We never sent a messenger before you without revealing to him: ‘There is no god but Me, so worship Me.’”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, upheld monotheism and taught it to his community. Thus, when a man said to him, “Whatever Allah wills and you will,” he replied, “Have you made me a rival to Allah? Rather, say: ‘Whatever Allah alone wills.’”

He also said: “Do not say, ‘Whatever Allah wills and Muhammad wills.’ Instead, say, ‘Whatever Allah wills, then Muhammad wills.’”

He forbade swearing by anyone other than Allah, saying: “Whoever wishes to swear an oath should swear by Allah or remain silent.”

---

## Page 1290

and he said:

“Whoever swears an oath by other than Allah has associated partners with Him.”

He also said:

“Do not praise me excessively as the Christians praised Jesus, son of Mary. I am only a servant, so say: ‘The servant of Allah and His Messenger.’”

For this reason, the scholars agree that no one may swear by any created thing, such as the Kaaba or anything similar.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade prostrating to him. When one of his Companions prostrated to him, he forbade him from doing so and said:

“Prostration is fitting only for Allah.”

He also said:

“If I were to command anyone to prostrate to another, I would command a woman to prostrate to her husband.”

He said to Mu‘ādh ibn Jabal, may Allah be pleased with him: “Tell me, if you passed by my grave, would you prostrate to it?” He replied, “No.” He said, “Then do not prostrate to me.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also forbade taking graves as places of prayer. During his final illness, he said:

“May Allah curse the Jews and Christians; they took the graves of their prophets as places of prayer.”

He was warning against what they had done. ‘Ā’ishah, may Allah be pleased with her, said: “Were it not for that, his grave would have been placed in the open, but he disliked its being made a place of prayer.”

It is also reported in the authentic hadith collection that, five days before his death, he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Those before you used to take graves as places of prayer. So do not make my house a place of recurring gatherings, nor make your houses graves. Invoke blessings upon me wherever you are, for your invocations of blessings reach me.”

For this reason, the leading scholars of Islam agree that building a mosque over graves is not prescribed, nor is praying beside graves prescribed. Indeed, many scholars hold that prayer performed beside them is invalid.

---

## Page 1291

The Sunnah regarding visits to Muslim graves is akin to offering the funeral prayer for them before burial. Allah Most High says in His Book concerning the hypocrites:

“Never offer prayer for any of them who dies, nor stand by his grave.”

The implied contrast indicates that funeral prayers are to be offered for believers and that one may stand by their graves.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, taught his Companions to say when visiting graves:

“Peace be upon you, inhabitants of the abode of believers. Allah willing, we shall join you. May Allah have mercy on those among us and you who have gone before, and those who come after. We ask Allah for well-being for ourselves and for you. O Allah, do not deprive us of the reward on their account, do not subject us to trials after them, and forgive us and them.”

This is because one of the greatest causes of idol worship was revering graves through acts of worship and the like. Allah Most High says in His Book:

“They said, ‘Do not abandon your gods; do not abandon Wadd, Suwā‘, Yaghūth, Ya‘ūq, or Nasr.’”

A group of the early generations said: These were the names of righteous men. When they died, people devoted themselves to remaining at their graves, then fashioned images of them and worshipped those images.

For this reason, the scholars agree that anyone who greets the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, at his grave must neither rub his burial chamber nor kiss it. Kissing and touching are reserved for the corners of Allah’s Sacred House; the house of a created being must not be treated like the House of the Creator. Likewise, it is the houses of Allah that are to be sought for circumambulation, prayer, and gatherings for worship: the mosques that Allah has permitted to be raised and His name to be mentioned therein. The houses of created beings must not be sought out and made places of recurring gatherings, as he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Do not make my house a place of recurring gatherings.”

All this is to establish

---

## Page 1292

pure monotheism, which is the foundation and foremost principle of religion. Allah accepts no deed without it; He forgives those who uphold it, but does not forgive those who abandon it. As Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whom He wills. Whoever associates partners with Allah has indeed committed a tremendous sin.”

For this reason, the declaration of monotheism is the finest and greatest of all speech. Thus, the greatest verse in the Quran is Āyat al-Kursī:

“Allah—there is no deity except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence. Neither drowsiness overtakes Him nor sleep.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever’s last words are ‘There is no deity except Allah’ will enter Paradise.”

A deity is one to whom the heart turns in worship, seeking help, placing its hope in him, fearing him, revering him, and honoring him.

---

## Page 1293

Section

This also includes moderation in following the Sunnah and adhering to it as it has come down—without addition or omission—as in discussions of (the Quran) and (the other attributes).

The position of the early generations of the Muslim community and Ahl al-Sunnah is that the Quran is the speech of Allah, revealed and uncreated. It originated from Him and will return to Him. More than one of the early scholars stated this. It is narrated through Sufyān ibn ʿUyaynah that ʿAmr ibn Dīnār—one of the eminent Successors—said: “I have always heard people saying this.”

The Quran that Allah revealed to His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is this very Quran that Muslims recite and write in their copies. It is Allah’s speech, not the speech of anyone else, even though His servants recite and convey it through their movements and voices. Speech is attributed to its original speaker, not to the person who conveys and transmits it. Allah Most High says:

“And if any of the polytheists seeks your protection, then grant him protection so that he may hear the speech of Allah. Then deliver him to his place of safety.”

This Quran is contained in the written copies, as Allah Most High says:

“Rather, it is a glorious Quran, in a Preserved Tablet.”

And Allah Most High says:

“Reciting purified pages, containing upright writings.”

And He says:

“Indeed, it is a noble Quran, in a well-guarded Book.”

The Quran is Allah’s speech in its letters, arrangement, and meanings. All of these are included in the Quran and in Allah’s speech. The vocalization of the letters is integral to their completeness, as the Prophet said

---

## Page 1294

, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Whoever recites the Quran with correct vocalization will receive ten good deeds for every letter.”

Abū Bakr and ʿUmar, may Allah be pleased with them both, said: “Preserving the Quran’s correct vocalization is dearer to us than preserving some of its letters.”

When Muslims write a copy of the Quran, they may leave it without letter dots or vowel marks if they wish. The Companions likewise wrote copies without dots or vowel marks, for they were Arabs who did not make grammatical errors. Such were the copies that ʿUthmān, may Allah be pleased with him, sent to the provincial cities during the time of the Successors. Then “grammatical errors” became widespread, so dots were added to the copies, and vowels were marked with red dots. Later, vowel marks resembling the written letters were used.

The scholars disagreed over whether this was disliked. Differing views are reported from Imam Ahmad—may Allah have mercy on him—and other scholars. Some held that it was disliked because it was an innovation; others held that it was not disliked because it was needed. Still others held that letter dots were disliked, but not vowel marks, since these clarify the vocalization. The correct view is that there is nothing wrong with it.

Also included is affirming what is established from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: that Allah speaks with a voice and calls to Adam, peace be upon him, with a voice, together with other hadiths of this kind. These, collectively, were the beliefs held by the early generations of the Muslim community and the leading scholars of the Sunnah.

The leading scholars of the Sunnah said:

“The Quran is the speech of Allah Most High, uncreated, wherever it is recited and wherever

---

## Page 1295

it is written.”

A servant’s recitation of the Quran should not be described as created, because that would include the revealed Quran; nor should it be described as uncreated, because that would include the servants’ actions.

None of the leading scholars of the early generations ever said that the servants’ voices when reciting the Quran are eternal. Rather, they condemned those who said, “A servant’s utterance of the Quran is uncreated.”

As for someone who says that the ink is eternal, he is among the most ignorant of people and the furthest removed from the Sunnah. Allah Most High says:

“Say, ‘If the sea were ink for the words of my Lord, the sea would be exhausted before the words of my Lord were exhausted, even if We brought another like it to replenish it.’”

Thus, He states that ink is used to write His words. Likewise, whoever says, “The Quran is not in the written copy; it contains only ink and paper, or an imitation and representation of it,” is a misguided innovator. Rather, the Quran that Allah revealed to Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is what lies between the two covers. The speech contained in the written copy—in the sense familiar to people—has a distinctive quality that sets it apart from all other things.

Likewise, whoever goes beyond the Sunnah and says that the servants’ utterances and voices are eternal is a misguided innovator, just like someone who says that Allah does not speak with letters or a voice: he too is an innovator who denies the Sunnah.

Likewise, whoever goes further and says that the ink is eternal is misguided, just like someone who says, “Allah’s speech is not contained in the written copies of the Quran.”

---

## Page 1296

As for those ignorant people who go still further and say, “The paper, leather, peg, and a piece of the wall are Allah’s speech,” they are like those who say, “Allah did not speak the Quran, nor is it His speech.” This excess in affirmation is the counterpart of outright denial, and both depart from the Sunnah and the united community.

Likewise, making the dots and vowel marks a separate subject of debate, whether to deny or affirm, is an innovation. This innovation arose a hundred years ago, or a little more. Whoever says, “The ink used to dot the letters and add vowel marks is eternal,” is misguided and ignorant. Whoever says, “The vocalization of the Quran’s letters is not part of the Quran,” is a misguided innovator.

Rather, what must be said is: “This Arabic Quran is Allah’s speech.” This includes its letters with their vocalization, just as it includes its meanings. It should also be said: “Everything between the two covers is Allah’s speech.” If the written copy of the Quran has dots and vowel marks, everything between its two covers is described, without qualification, as Allah’s speech. If it has neither dots nor vowel marks, like the early copies written by the Companions, what lies between its two covers is likewise Allah’s speech.

It is therefore impermissible to sow discord among Muslims over a newly introduced matter and a merely verbal dispute with no substantive basis. Nor is it permissible to introduce into the religion anything that does not belong to it.

---

## Page 1297

Section

Likewise, moderation and balance must be observed regarding the Companions and the Prophet’s relatives—may Allah be pleased with them. Allah Most High praised His Prophet’s Companions—both the forerunners and those who followed them in righteousness. He declared that He is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him, and mentioned them in verses of His Book, such as His words:

“Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah, and those with him are firm against the disbelievers and merciful among themselves. You see them bowing and prostrating, seeking Allah’s bounty and pleasure. Their mark is on their faces from the traces of prostration. That is their description in the Torah. Their description in the Gospel is like a plant that sends out its shoots, which strengthen it, so it grows thick and stands firmly on its stalks, delighting the sowers—so that through them He may enrage the disbelievers. Allah has promised those among them who believe and do righteous deeds forgiveness and a tremendous reward.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Allah was certainly pleased with the believers when they pledged allegiance to you beneath the tree. He knew what was in their hearts, so He sent down tranquillity upon them and rewarded them with a victory near at hand.”

The authentic hadith collections report that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Do not revile my Companions. By Him in Whose Hand is my soul, if any of you were to spend gold equal to Mount Uhud, it would not equal a mudd (a small measure) given by one of them, nor even half of it.”

The adherents of the Sunnah and the united community unanimously agree on what has been transmitted through numerous independent chains from the Commander of the Faithful, Alī ibn Abī

---

## Page 1298

Tālib—may Allah be pleased with him—who said:

“The best of this nation after its Prophet is Abū Bakr, then Umar—may Allah be pleased with them both.”

The Companions of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, agreed to pledge allegiance to Uthmān after Umar—may Allah be pleased with them both. It is established that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The Prophetic caliphate will last thirty years, then become kingship.”

He also said, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Follow my Sunnah and the Sunnah of the rightly guided caliphs after me. Hold fast to it and cling to it with all your strength. Beware of newly introduced matters, for every innovation is misguidance.”

The Commander of the Faithful, Alī ibn Abī Tālib—may Allah be pleased with him—was the last of the rightly guided caliphs.

The general body of the adherents of the Sunnah—scholars, devout worshippers, rulers, and soldiers—have agreed in saying: Abū Bakr, then Umar, then Uthmān, then Alī—may Allah be pleased with them all. The evidence for this and the virtues of the Companions are abundant; this is not the place to discuss them.

Likewise, we believe in “exercising restraint regarding the disputes that arose among them,” and we know that some of what has been reported concerning those disputes is false. They exercised independent judgment: either they were correct and earned two rewards, or they were rewarded for their righteous efforts and forgiven their errors. Whatever sins they committed—Allah having already promised them the finest reward—Allah forgives them through repentance, good deeds that erase sins, calamities that expiate them, or other means.

They are the best generation of this nation, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The best of generations is my generation, among whom I was sent, then those who follow them.”

And this is the best nation brought forth for mankind.

---

## Page 1299

And yet we know that Alī ibn Abī Tālib—may Allah be pleased with him—was more virtuous and closer to the truth than Muāwiya and those who joined him in fighting Alī. This is based on the report established in both Sahīh collections from Abū Saīd al-Khudrī—may Allah be pleased with him—that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“A group will break away from the religion at a time of division among the Muslims. They will be killed by whichever of the two sides is closer to the truth.”

This hadith indicates that each side possessed some truth, and that Alī—may Allah be pleased with him—was closer to the truth. As for those who refrained from fighting during the civil strife, such as Sad ibn Abī Waqqās, Ibn Umar, and others—may Allah be pleased with them—they followed the teachings they had heard concerning fighting during civil strife. This is also the position of most scholars of hadith.

Likewise, “the household of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace,” has rights that must be upheld. Allah has granted them an entitlement to the one-fifth share and to property acquired without fighting, and has commanded that blessings be invoked upon them along with blessings upon the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. The Messenger told us:

“Say: ‘O Allah, bestow blessings upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, as You bestowed blessings upon the family of Abraham. Indeed, You are Praiseworthy, Glorious. And grant Your blessings to Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, as You granted Your blessings to the family of Abraham. Indeed, You are Praiseworthy, Glorious.’”

The family of Muhammad are those who are forbidden to receive charity. This is what al-Shāfiī, Ahmad ibn Hanbal, and other scholars—may Allah have mercy on them—have stated, for the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Charity is not lawful for Muhammad or for the family of Muhammad.”

Allah Most High has said in His Book:

“Allah intends only to remove impurity from you, O members of the household, and to purify you completely.”

Allah has forbidden them to receive charity because it is

---

## Page 1300

people’s impurities. Some of the early Muslims said:

“Love for Abū Bakr and Umar is faith; hatred of them is hypocrisy.”

The Musnad and Sunan collections report that when al-Abbās complained to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, about the cold treatment some people showed them, he said:

“By Him in whose hand is my soul, they will not enter Paradise until they love you for my sake.”

It is also reported in the Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah chose the descendants of Ishmael; He chose Banū Kināna from the descendants of Ishmael; He chose Quraysh from Kināna; He chose Banū Hāshim from Quraysh; and He chose me from Banū Hāshim.”

When civil strife erupted with the killing of Uthmān and the subsequent division of the Muslim community, some who loved Uthmān and went to excess in their devotion to him turned against Alī—may Allah be pleased with him. Such were many of the people of Syria who, at that time, reviled Alī—may Allah be pleased with him—and hated him. Likewise, some who loved Alī—may Allah be pleased with him—and went to excess in their devotion to him turned against Uthmān—may Allah be pleased with him. Such were many of the people of Iraq who hated and reviled Uthmān—may Allah be pleased with him. Their religious innovation subsequently grew more extreme, until they reviled Abū Bakr and Umar—may Allah be pleased with them both—and the affliction they caused then intensified.

The Sunnah is to love both Uthmān and Alī, and to give Abū Bakr and Umar precedence over them—may Allah be pleased

---

## Page 1301

with them—because of the virtues Allah bestowed upon those two, by which they surpassed both Uthmān and Alī. In His Book, Allah has forbidden division and disunity and commanded us to hold fast to His rope. This is therefore a matter in which the believer must exercise care and hold fast to the rope of Allah, for the Sunnah rests upon knowledge, justice, and adherence to the Book of Allah and the Sunnah of His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

When the Rāfidites began reviling “the Companions,” the scholars began calling for the punishment of anyone who reviled the Companions. Then the Rāfidites declared the Companions unbelievers and made statements about them; elsewhere, we have explained the ruling that applies to them for these statements. At that time, no one was discussing “Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya,” nor was discussion of him a matter of religion. Later, certain developments occurred, and some people began openly cursing Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. Their purpose may have been to use this as a way to curse others. Most adherents of the Sunnah disapproved of cursing any specific individual. Some people who professed adherence to the Sunnah heard of this and came to believe that Yazīd was among the foremost righteous men and leaders of right guidance.

Those who went to extremes concerning him came to occupy two opposite poles. One side says that he was an unbeliever and a heretic, and that he killed the son of the daughter of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and killed the Ansār and their sons at al-Harra to avenge members of his family who had been killed as unbelievers, such as his maternal grandfather, Utba ibn Rabīʿa, his maternal uncle, al-Walīd, and others. They also relate various accounts of his notoriety for drinking wine and openly committing acts of indecency.

---

## Page 1302

And other people believe that he was a just leader who guided others and was himself rightly guided, that he was one of the Companions—or even one of their foremost members—and that he was among the friends of Allah Most High. Some may even believe that he was one of the prophets. They say: “Whoever withholds judgment concerning Yazīd, Allah will make him stand over the fire of Hell.”

They also relate, on the authority of Shaykh “Hasan ibn Adī,” that there were so-and-so many saints, and that those who withheld judgment concerning him stood over the Fire because of what they said about Yazīd. In Shaykh Hasan’s time, they introduced falsehoods in both verse and prose. They went to extremes concerning Shaykh Adī “and concerning Yazīd,” making claims contrary to the path followed by Shaykh Adī the Elder—may Allah sanctify his soul. His path was sound and contained none of these religious innovations. They were afflicted by Rāfidites who opposed them and killed Shaykh Hasan, and turmoil arose that neither Allah nor His Messenger loves.

These extremes concerning Yazīd, on both sides, contradict the consensus of the people of knowledge and faith. Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya was born during the caliphate of Uthmān ibn Affān—may Allah be pleased with him. He did not live in the Prophet’s lifetime, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and was not one of the Companions, by the agreement of the scholars. Nor was he known for religious devotion and righteousness. He was one of the young Muslims, neither an unbeliever nor a heretic. He assumed rule after his father, with some Muslims disapproving and others approving. He possessed courage and generosity, and he did not openly commit acts of indecency as his opponents claim.

Grave events occurred during his rule:

- One was the killing of al-Husayn—may Allah be pleased with him. He did not order al-Husayn’s killing, nor did he display

---

## Page 1303

joy at his killing; nor did he tap his front teeth with a rod—may Allah be pleased with him—nor did he have al-Husayn’s head—may Allah be pleased with him—taken to Syria. He did, however, order that al-Husayn—may Allah be pleased with him—be stopped and prevented from taking power, even if this required fighting him. His deputies exceeded his orders, and Shamir ibn Dhī al-Jawshan urged Ubayd Allah ibn Ziyād to kill him. Ubayd Allah ibn Ziyād then acted unjustly against him.
