Al-Husayn—may Allah be pleased with him—asked them to let him go to Yazīd, serve as a frontier guard, or return to Mecca. They refused him these options—may Allah be pleased with him—and demanded that he surrender to them as a captive. Ubayd Allah ordered Umar ibn Saʿd to fight him and a group of his family—may Allah be pleased with them—and they killed him unjustly.

His killing—may Allah be pleased with him—was a tremendous calamity. The killing of al-Husayn, and that of Uthmān before him, were among the greatest causes of turmoil in this community, and their killers are among the worst of creation in Allah’s sight.

When their family members—may Allah be pleased with them—came to Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya, he treated them honourably and sent them to Medina. It is reported that he cursed Ibn Ziyād for killing al-Husayn and said: “I would have been content with the obedience of the people of Iraq without al-Husayn being killed.”

Yet, despite this, he showed no disapproval of his killing. Defending his cause and avenging his death were duties incumbent upon him. Thus, those committed to the truth reproached him for neglecting his duty, in addition to other matters. His opponents, however, add fabricated accusations against him.

---

## Page 1304

As for the second matter:

The people of the Prophet’s city, Medina, revoked their pledge of allegiance to him and expelled his deputies and family. He sent an army against them, ordering it, if they did not submit to him after three days, to enter the city by force of arms and give the troops free rein there for three days. His soldiers thus spent three days in the Prophet’s city killing, plundering, and deflowering women whom they had no right to touch. He then sent an army to honoured Mecca, which they besieged. Yazīd died while they were still besieging Mecca. These were acts of aggression and injustice committed on his orders.

For this reason, the position held by Ahl al-Sunnah and the leading scholars of the Muslim community was that he should neither be reviled nor loved. Sālih ibn Ahmad ibn Hanbal said:

“I said to my father: ‘Some people say that they love Yazīd.’

“He replied: ‘My son, would anyone who believes in Allah and the Last Day love Yazīd?’

“I asked: ‘Father, why, then, do you not curse him?’

“He replied: ‘My son, when have you ever seen your father curse anyone?’”

It is also reported that he was asked: “Would you record hadith transmitted by Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya?”

He replied: “No, certainly not! Was he not the one who did what he did to the people of Medina?”

Thus, the leading Muslim scholars regard Yazīd as merely one king among others. They do not love him as they love the righteous and the allies of Allah, nor do they revile him, for they do not approve of cursing a particular Muslim. This is because al-Bukhārī narrated in his Sahīh, on the authority of Umar ibn al-Khattāb—may Allah be pleased with him:

“There was a man known as Himār (‘Donkey’) who frequently drank wine. Whenever he was brought before the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—he would beat him. Then said

---

## Page 1305

a man: ‘May Allah curse him! How often he is brought before the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace!’ The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said: ‘Do not curse him, for he loves Allah and His Messenger.’”

Nevertheless, some among Ahl al-Sunnah permit cursing Yazīd because they believe that he committed acts of injustice whose perpetrator may rightly be cursed. Others hold that he should be loved because he was a Muslim who assumed rule during the time of the Companions, and the Companions pledged allegiance to him. They say: “The reports about him have not been authenticated; he had good qualities, or he was exercising independent judgment in what he did.”

The correct position is that held by the leading scholars: he should neither be singled out for love nor cursed. Nevertheless, if he was sinful or unjust, Allah forgives the sinful and the unjust, especially when they have performed great good deeds.

Al-Bukhārī narrated in his Sahīh, on the authority of Ibn Umar—may Allah be pleased with them both—that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“The first army to attack Constantinople will be forgiven.”

The first army to attack it was commanded by Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya, and Abū Ayyūb al-Ansārī—may Allah be pleased with him—was with him.

Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya may be confused with his paternal uncle, Yazīd ibn Abī Sufyān. Yazīd ibn Abī Sufyān was one of the Companions, among their finest, and the best of the family of Harb. He was one of the commanders whom Abū Bakr—may Allah be pleased with him—sent to conquer Syria. Abū Bakr walked alongside his mount, giving him advice as he saw him off. Yazīd said to him:

“O successor to the Messenger of Allah, either you ride or I dismount.”

He replied:

“I will not ride, and you will not dismount. I seek reward for these steps of mine

---

## Page 1306

in Allah’s cause.”

When he died after the conquests of Syria, during Umar’s caliphate, Umar—may Allah be pleased with him—appointed his brother Muʿāwiya in his place. Yazīd was born to Muʿāwiya during the caliphate of Uthmān ibn Affān. Muʿāwiya remained in Syria until the events in question took place.

One must therefore leave the matter at that and refrain from discussing Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya or testing Muslims by their stance toward him. This is a religious innovation contrary to the way of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamāʿah. Because of this, some ignorant people have come to believe that Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya was one of the Companions and among the foremost righteous men and just rulers. This is a clear error.

---

## Page 1307

Section

The same applies to dividing the Muslim community and testing its members on matters that neither Allah nor His Messenger has commanded—for example, saying to a man, “You are a Shakīlī or a Qarfandī.” These are false names for which Allah has sent down no authority. Neither Shakīlī nor Qarfandī appears in Allah’s Book, in the Sunnah of His Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—or in the well-known reports from the early imams.

When a Muslim is asked about this, he must say: “I am neither a Shakīlī nor a Qarfandī. Rather, I am a Muslim who follows Allah’s Book and the Sunnah of His Messenger.”

We have transmitted a report from Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān that he asked Abdullāh ibn Abbās—may Allah be pleased with them both—“Do you follow the religion of Alī or the religion of Uthmān?”

He replied: “I follow neither the religion of Alī nor the religion of Uthmān. Rather, I follow the religion of Allah’s Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace.”

Likewise, the righteous predecessors would all say: “All these sectarian ways lead to the Fire.” One of them would say: “I am not concerned with which of the two blessings is greater: that Allah guided me to Islam, or that He kept me away from these sectarian ways.”

Allah Most High has called us Muslims, believers, and servants of Allah in the Quran. We must therefore not turn away from the names Allah has given us to names introduced by certain people—names they and their forefathers have devised—for which Allah has sent down no authority.

---

## Page 1308

Even names that may legitimately be used—whether indicating affiliation with an imam, such as Hanafī, Mālikī, Shāfi‘ī, or Hanbalī; with a shaykh, such as Qādirī, Adawī, and the like; with tribes, such as Qaysī and Yamānī; or with regions, such as Syrian, Iraqi, and Egyptian—must not be used to test people or as grounds for loyalty or enmity. Rather, the most honoured people in Allah’s sight are those most mindful of Him, whatever group they belong to.

Allah’s true close friends are those who believe and are mindful of Him. He—glorified be He—has stated that His close friends are the believers who fear Him. He has explained who these God-fearing people are in His words, Most High:

“Righteousness is not that you turn your faces towards the east or the west. Rather, righteousness is to believe in Allah, the Last Day, the angels, the Book, and the prophets; to give wealth, despite loving it, to relatives, orphans, the needy, stranded travellers, those who ask for help, and to free slaves; to establish prayer and give zakat; to fulfil pledges when made; and to remain patient in poverty, hardship, and times of battle. These are the ones who are true, and these are the God-fearing.”

Being mindful of Allah means doing what Allah has commanded and refraining from what He has forbidden.

The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—described the condition of Allah’s close friends and how they attained that status. In Sahīh al-Bukhārī, Abū Hurayra—may Allah be pleased with him—narrates that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Allah—Blessed and Exalted—says: ‘Whoever shows hostility to a close friend of Mine has openly challenged Me

---

## Page 1309

to war. My servant draws near to Me through nothing like fulfilling what I have made obligatory upon him. My servant continues to draw near to Me through voluntary acts of worship until I love him. When I love him, I become his hearing with which he hears, his sight with which he sees, his hand with which he grasps, and his foot with which he walks. Through Me he hears, through Me he sees, through Me he grasps, and through Me he walks. If he asks Me, I will surely give to him; if he seeks My protection, I will surely protect him. I have never hesitated over anything I do as I hesitate over taking the soul of My believing servant: he dislikes death, and I dislike causing him distress, yet he must undergo it.’”

This hadith thus describes two levels of drawing near to Allah Most High:

(The first): drawing near to Him through obligatory acts of worship.

(The second): drawing near to Allah through voluntary acts of worship after fulfilling the obligations.

The first is the rank of “those who follow the middle course”—the righteous, those on the right. The second is the rank of the believers who are “foremost,” as Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, the righteous will be in bliss, gazing from their couches. You will recognize in their faces the radiance of bliss. They will be given pure, sealed wine to drink, its seal being musk. For this, let those who strive compete.”

Ibn ʿAbbās—may Allah be pleased with them both—said: “It is mixed for those on the right, while those brought near drink it pure.” Allah has mentioned this meaning in several places in His Book. Thus, everyone who believes in Allah and His Messenger and is mindful of Allah is one of Allah’s close friends.

---

## Page 1310

And Allah, glorified be He, has made it obligatory for believers to show loyalty to one another, and has required them to bear enmity towards the disbelievers. He Most High says:

“O you who believe, do not take the Jews and Christians as allies. They are allies of one another. Whoever among you takes them as allies is indeed one of them. Indeed, Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people.”

“So you see those in whose hearts is disease hastening to ally themselves with them, saying, ‘We fear that a reversal of fortune may strike us.’ But perhaps Allah will bring victory or some decree from Himself, and they will then regret what they concealed within themselves.”

“And those who believe will say, ‘Are these the ones who swore their strongest oaths by Allah that they were indeed with you?’ Their deeds have become worthless, and they have become losers.”

“O you who believe, whoever among you turns back from his religion—Allah will bring forth a people whom He loves and who love Him, humble towards the believers, firm towards the disbelievers, striving in Allah’s cause and fearing no blame from anyone. That is Allah’s bounty, which He grants to whom He wills. And Allah is all-Encompassing, all-Knowing.”

“Your ally is none other than Allah, His Messenger, and those who believe—those who establish prayer and give zakah, and who bow in worship.”

“And whoever takes Allah, His Messenger, and those who believe as allies—indeed, the party of Allah will be victorious.”

He, glorified be He, has thus declared that the believer’s allies are Allah, His Messenger, and His believing servants. This applies to every believer possessing this quality, whether or not he belongs to a particular affiliation, town, school, or spiritual path. Allah Most High says:

“The believing men and believing women are allies of one another.”

And He Most High says:

“Indeed, those who believed, emigrated, and strove with their wealth and their lives in Allah’s cause, and those who gave shelter and support—they are allies of one another.”

up to

---

## Page 1311

His words:

“And those who believed afterwards, emigrated, and strove alongside you—they are of you.”

And He Most High says:

“If two groups of believers fight one another,”

up to His words, Most High:

“Then reconcile them with justice and act equitably. Indeed, Allah loves those who act equitably.”

“The believers are but brothers, so reconcile your brothers and fear Allah, that you may receive mercy.”

In the collections of authentic hadith, it is reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The believers, in their mutual love, mercy, and compassion, are like a single body: when one limb suffers, the rest of the body responds with fever and sleeplessness.”

These collections also report that he said:

“One believer is to another like a building whose parts strengthen one another.” He then interlaced his fingers.

They also report that he said:

“By Him in whose hand is my soul, none of you believes until he loves for his brother what he loves for himself.”

And he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“A Muslim is the brother of another Muslim: he neither abandons him nor wrongs him.”

There are many similar texts in the Quran and Sunnah. In them, Allah has made His believing servants allies of one another and brothers, supporting one another and showing one another mercy and compassion. He, glorified be He, has commanded them to unite and forbidden them to divide and disagree. He says:

“And hold firmly to the rope of Allah all together, and do not become divided.”

And He says:

“Indeed, those who divided their religion and became sects—you have nothing to do with them. Their affair rests only with Allah,”

and the remainder of the verse.

How, then, in light of this, can it be permissible for the community of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to become divided and fall into disagreement

---

## Page 1312

to the point that a man allies himself with one group and shows hostility towards another on the basis of conjecture and desire, without any proof from Allah Most High? Allah has declared His Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, free of any association with those who act in this way. Such is the conduct of the people of innovation, like the Khawārij, who broke away from the Muslim community and deemed it lawful to shed the blood of those who disagreed with them. As for the people of the Sunnah and the united community, they hold firmly to the rope of Allah.

At the very least, such conduct involves a man favouring someone who agrees with his own desires, even though someone else may be more mindful of Allah. Rather, one must give precedence to those whom Allah and His Messenger have given precedence, and place after them those whom Allah and His Messenger have placed after them; love what Allah and His Messenger love, hate what Allah and His Messenger hate, forbid what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden, and be pleased with what Allah and His Messenger are pleased with. Muslims must stand united.

How much worse, then, when some people go so far as to declare another person misguided and an unbeliever, even though that person may be in the right and his position may accord with the Quran and Sunnah? Even if his Muslim brother has made a mistake in some religious matter, not everyone who makes a mistake is an unbeliever or a sinner. Indeed, Allah has pardoned this community for mistakes and forgetfulness.

“Allah Most High has said in His Book, in the supplication of the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and the believers:

‘Our Lord, do not impose blame upon us if we have forgotten or erred.’

And it is authentically reported that Allah said: ‘I have done so.’”

This is especially so when someone may agree with you in something more specific than Islam—for example, when he, like you, is

---

## Page 1313

a follower of al-Shāfiʿī’s school “or affiliated with Shaykh 'Adī,” yet subsequently disagrees on some matter—and he may well be right. How, then, can it be deemed lawful to violate his honour, shed his blood, or take his property, despite what Allah Most High has stated concerning the rights of Muslims and believers? How can it be permissible to divide the community through invented labels that have no basis in the Book of Allah or the Sunnah of His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace? This division within the community—among its scholars, shaykhs, rulers, and leading figures—is what has enabled its enemies to gain power over it.

This has happened because they have ceased to act in obedience to Allah and His Messenger. As Allah Most High says:

“And from those who say, ‘We are Christians,’ We took their covenant; but they forgot a portion of that of which they were reminded. So We caused animosity and hatred among them.”

Whenever people abandon some of what Allah has commanded them, animosity and hatred arise among them. When people become divided, they fall into corruption and perish; when they unite, their affairs are set right and they attain power. Unity is a mercy, while division is a punishment.

All of this is encompassed in enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong, as Allah Most High says:

“O you who believe, fear Allah as He should be feared, and do not die except as Muslims.”

“And hold firmly to the rope of Allah all together, and do not become divided.”

And further on, He says:

“And let there be among you a group calling to goodness, enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong. It is they who are successful.”

Thus, enjoining what is right includes calling for harmony and unity, and forbidding

---

## Page 1314

discord and division. Forbidding what is wrong also includes enforcing legal penalties against those who depart from the law of Allah Most High.

Whoever believes that a human being is a god, invokes a deceased person, asks him for provision, victory or guidance, places his trust in him, or prostrates before him is to be called upon to repent; if he does not repent, he is to be beheaded. Whoever regards any of the “shaykhs” as superior to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, or believes that anyone can dispense with obedience to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is to be called upon to repent; if he does not repent, he is to be beheaded.

Likewise, whoever believes that any of the “friends of Allah” can stand in relation to Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as al-Khidr stood in relation to Moses, peace be upon him, is to be called upon to repent; if he does not repent, he is to be beheaded. This is because al-Khidr was not a member of Moses’s community, peace be upon him, nor was he obliged to obey him. Rather, he said to him:

“I possess some knowledge from Allah which He has taught me and which you do not know; and you possess some knowledge from Allah which He has taught you and which I do not know.”

Moses was sent to the Children of Israel, as our Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Each prophet used to be sent specifically to his own people, whereas I have been sent to all mankind.”

Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was sent to both mankind and the jinn. Thus, whoever believes that anyone may depart from his law and from obedience to him is a disbeliever who must be put to death.

---

## Page 1315

Likewise, whoever declares Muslims disbelievers or deems it lawful to shed their blood and take their property on the basis of an innovation he has introduced, which is found neither in the Book of Allah nor in the Sunnah of His Messenger, must be forbidden from doing so and punished in a manner that deters him, even by putting him to death or fighting him. When transgressors from all groups are punished and the devout from all groups are honoured, this is among the greatest means of pleasing Allah and His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and setting the affairs of the Muslims right.

Those in authority—the scholars, leaders and shaykhs of every group—must oversee their people, enjoin what is right and forbid what is wrong. They must command them to do what Allah and His Messenger have commanded and forbid them from doing what Allah and His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, have forbidden.

(The former includes the prescribed observances of Islam: the five daily prayers at their appointed times; establishing Friday prayer and congregational prayers, both obligatory and regularly observed Sunnah prayers, such as the Eid prayers, the eclipse prayer, the prayer for rain, the tarāwīh prayers, funeral prayers and others. It also includes prescribed charity, prescribed fasting and pilgrimage to the Sacred House.

It likewise includes belief in Allah, His angels, His Books, His messengers and the Last Day, and belief in divine decree, both its good and its evil; and excellence in worship, which is to worship Allah as though you see Him, for even though you do not see Him, He sees you.

It also includes everything else that Allah and His Messenger have commanded, inwardly and outwardly, such as devoting one’s religion sincerely to Allah, placing one’s trust in Allah, and loving Allah and His Messenger more than

---

## Page 1316

anyone or anything else; hoping for Allah’s mercy; fearing His punishment; patiently accepting Allah’s judgment; and submitting to His command. It also includes speaking truthfully, fulfilling covenants, returning trusts to their rightful owners, honouring parents, maintaining family ties, cooperating in righteousness and piety, treating neighbours, orphans, the needy, wayfarers, companions, wives and slaves with kindness, and being just in word and deed. Then comes encouraging noble character, such as maintaining ties with those who sever them with you, giving to those who withhold from you, and pardoning those who wrong you. Allah Most High says:

“The recompense for an evil deed is an evil deed like it, but whoever pardons and makes reconciliation—his reward is with Allah. Indeed, He does not love the wrongdoers.”

“As for those who defend themselves after being wronged, there is no cause to blame them.”

“Blame lies only with those who wrong people and transgress unjustly in the land. They will suffer a painful punishment.”

“But whoever is patient and forgives—that is indeed a matter requiring firm resolve.”

As for the “wrong” that Allah and His Messenger have forbidden, the gravest is associating partners with Allah: invoking another deity alongside Allah, whether the sun, the moon, the stars, an angel, a prophet, a righteous man, one of the jinn, images of these beings, their graves, or anything else that is invoked besides Allah Most High, appealed to for deliverance, or prostrated before. All this and anything like it constitute the association of partners with Allah, which He has forbidden through all His messengers.

Allah has also forbidden taking a life without right and consuming people’s wealth unjustly, whether by

---

## Page 1317

wrongful seizure, usury, or gambling, as in the sales and transactions forbidden by the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him. He has likewise forbidden severing family ties, mistreating parents, giving short measure and weight, sin, and unjust aggression.

Allah Most High has also forbidden anyone to say about Him what he does not know. This includes relating reports from Allah and His Messenger and confidently asserting their truth without knowing whether they are authentic, or describing Allah with attributes unsupported by any Book revealed by Allah or any knowledge transmitted from the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him. This applies whether such descriptions involve negation and denial of His attributes—as when the Jahmiyyah claim that He is neither above the Throne nor above the heavens, that He will not be seen in the Hereafter, and that He neither speaks nor loves, along with similar claims by which they deny what Allah and His Messenger have said—or involve affirming attributes that liken Him to His creation. Examples of the latter include claiming that He walks upon the earth, sits with His creatures, that they see Him with their own eyes, that the heavens contain and encompass Him, or that He has moved among His creatures, and other kinds of falsehood fabricated against Allah.

Likewise forbidden are innovated acts of worship that Allah and His Messenger, peace and blessings be upon him, have not prescribed. As Allah Most High says:

“Or have they partners who have ordained for them a religion which Allah has not permitted?”

Allah has prescribed acts of worship for His believing servants, but Satan has devised acts of worship for them that imitate these. For example, Allah prescribed that they worship Him alone, without any partner; Satan prescribed that they associate partners with Him—that is, worship others besides Him and associate them with Him. Allah also prescribed the five daily prayers, reciting the Quran during them, and listening

---

## Page 1318

to it, and gathering to listen to the Quran outside prayer as well. In the first surah He revealed to His Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, He commanded recitation at its beginning:

“Read in the name of your Lord who created.”

And at its end He commanded prostration, saying:

“Prostrate and draw near.”

For this reason, the greatest form of remembrance in prayer is reciting the Quran, and the greatest physical act is prostrating to Allah alone, without any partner. Allah Most High says:

“And the recitation at dawn—indeed, the recitation at dawn is ever witnessed.”

He also says:

“When the Quran is recited, listen to it and remain silent so that you may receive mercy.”

When the Companions of the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, gathered, they would ask one of them to recite while the others listened. Umar ibn al-Khattāb would say to Abū Mūsā, may Allah be pleased with them both, “Remind us of our Lord.” He would then recite while they listened.

“The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, passed by Abū Mūsā, may Allah be pleased with him, while he was reciting, and began listening to his recitation. He said, ‘O Abū Mūsā, I passed by you last night and began listening to your recitation.’ He replied, ‘Had I known, I would have made my recitation especially beautiful for you.’ He also said, ‘Allah is indeed more attentive—that is, listens more closely—to a man who recites the Quran in a beautiful voice than the owner of a singing slave girl is to her.’”

Such was the listening practiced by the believers, the early generations of the Muslim community, and the leading shaykhs, such as Ma‘rūf al-Karkhī, al-Fudayl ibn ‘Iyād, Abū Sulaymān al-Dārānī, and others like them. It was also the listening practiced by the shaykhs

---

## Page 1319

of the later generations who were prominent, such as Shaykh ‘Abd al-Qādir, Shaykh ‘Adī ibn Musāfir, Shaykh Abū Madyan, and other shaykhs—may Allah have mercy on them. As for the polytheists, their listening was as Allah Most High described it in His Book, saying:

“And their prayer at the House was nothing but whistling and clapping.”

The early generations said that mukā’ means whistling, and tasdiya means clapping the hands. The polytheists would gather in the Sacred Mosque, clapping and making sounds, treating this as worship and prayer. Allah therefore condemned them for it and counted it among the false practices He forbade.

Whoever treats a similar kind of listening as worship and a devotional act through which to draw near to Allah has resembled these people in some of their practices. Nor was this practiced by the three generations praised by the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, or by the leading shaykhs.

As for listening to singing for amusement, this is particular to festive occasions for women and children, as the transmitted reports indicate. The religion of Islam allows ample scope and entails no hardship. The mainstay of the religion, without which it cannot stand, is the five obligatory prayers. Muslims must give them a degree of care that is not required for anything else.

Umar ibn al-Khattāb, may Allah be pleased with him, used to write to his governors:

“The most important of your affairs in my view is prayer. Whoever safeguards it and observes it consistently safeguards his religion. Whoever neglects it will be even more neglectful of his other deeds.”

---

## Page 1320

Prayer is the first act of worship Allah made obligatory. Allah Himself prescribed the five prayers by addressing His Messenger on the night of the Ascension. It was also the last thing the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, enjoined upon his community as he was departing this world. He kept saying:

“Observe prayer, observe prayer, and care for those whom you own.”

Prayer is the first of a servant’s deeds for which he will be called to account, and the last thing to be lost from the religion. When it is gone, the entire religion is gone. It is the pillar of the religion: when it is gone, the religion collapses. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“The essence of the matter is Islam, its pillar is prayer, and its highest peak is jihad in the path of Allah.”

Allah has said in His Book:

“But there came after them successors who neglected prayer and followed their desires; so they will meet ruin.”

Abdullāh ibn Masʿūd, may Allah be pleased with him, and others said: “Neglecting it means delaying it beyond its appointed time. Had they abandoned it, they would have been disbelievers.”

Allah Most High said:

“Guard your prayers, especially the middle prayer.”

Guarding them means performing them at their appointed times. Allah Most High also said:

“So woe to those who pray, yet are heedless of their prayer.”

These are those who delay their prayers until their appointed time has passed. Muslims have agreed that it is not permissible to delay a daytime prayer until the night, or a nighttime prayer until the day—not for a traveller, a sick person, or anyone else. When there is a need, however, a Muslim may combine the two daytime prayers—Zuhr and ʿAsr—at the time of either, and combine the two nighttime prayers—Maghrib and ʿIshā’—at the time of either. This applies, for example, to travellers and the sick, and in cases of rain and similar valid excuses.

---

## Page 1321

Allah has required Muslims to pray according to their ability, as Allah Most High says:

“So fear Allah as much as you are able.”

A man must therefore pray with complete ritual purification, complete recitation, and complete bowing and prostration. If he has no water, or using it would harm him because of illness, cold, or some other reason, and he is in a state of minor or major ritual impurity, he must perform dry purification with clean earth—that is, soil—wiping his face and hands with it, and then pray. He must not delay the prayer beyond its appointed time, by agreement of the scholars. Likewise, if he is imprisoned, restrained, chronically disabled, or otherwise impeded, he must pray as his circumstances permit.

If he is facing an enemy, he must likewise perform the prayer of fear. Allah Most High says:

“When you travel through the land, there is no blame upon you for shortening the prayer if you fear that those who disbelieve may attack you. Indeed, the disbelievers are your open enemies.”

“And when you are among them and lead them in prayer, let a group of them stand with you,”

up to His words:

“And let them take their precautions and carry their weapons,”

and then to His words:

“But when you are secure, establish prayer. Indeed, prayer has been prescribed for the believers at appointed times.”

Muslims who are able to do so must instruct everyone—men, women, and even children—to pray. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Command them to pray at seven, strike them for neglecting it at ten, and separate them in their beds.”

If an adult man refuses to perform even one of the five prayers, or omits any of its obligatory elements whose obligatory status is agreed upon, he must be called upon to repent and, unless he repents, put to death. Among the scholars are those who

---

## Page 1322

say that he is a disbelieving apostate: no funeral prayer is to be offered for him, nor is he to be buried among Muslims. Others say that he is like a highway robber, a murderer, or someone who commits unlawful sexual intercourse after having consummated a valid marriage.

The tremendous importance of prayer deserves mention here, for it is the foundation and pillar of the religion. Allah Most High accords it greater honour in His Book than any other act of worship. Sometimes He singles it out for mention; sometimes He associates it with zakat, with patience, or with sacrifice, as in His words:

“Establish prayer and give zakat.”

And His words:

“Seek help through patience and prayer.”

And His words:

“So pray to your Lord and sacrifice.”

And His words:

“Indeed, my prayer, my sacrifice, my living and my dying are for Allah, Lord of the worlds. He has no partner. This I have been commanded, and I am the first of the Muslims.”

Sometimes He begins and ends His enumeration of righteous deeds with prayer, as in the surah “A Questioner Asked” and at the beginning of the surah “The Believers.” Allah Most High says:

“Successful indeed are the believers,
those who are humble in their prayers,
who turn away from idle talk,
who give zakat,
who guard their private parts,
except with their spouses or those whom their right hands possess, for then they are free from blame.
But whoever seeks beyond that—those are the transgressors.
And those who honour their trusts and covenants,
and who carefully maintain their prayers.
Those are the inheritors,
who will inherit Paradise; they will abide therein forever.”

We ask Allah the Magnificent to make us and you among those who inherit Paradise and abide therein forever. Peace be upon you, and Allah’s mercy and blessings. All praise belongs to Allah alone. May Allah bestow blessings and abundant peace upon Muhammad, his family, and his Companions.
